Fix: `el.removeAttribute('style')` (or toggle a class) instead of `el.style.display = '...'`, so the CSS media query regains control for the rest of the session.
- **A headless-browser render that looks perfect can still miss a real-device-only bug, and vice versa — always root-cause which one you're looking at before "fixing" it.** Two cases this session: (1) the "Knock, we'll answer" button's corner-clipping only exists on a real iPhone's physically-rounded display, confirmed by first proving zero DOM overflow in Chromium — the fix (more margin) was still correct, but guessing "it must be a layout bug" first would have wasted time hunting for a CSS overflow that didn't exist. (2) A seemingly reproducible drawer-width discrepancy between `account.html` (70px inset, 26px radius) and `products.html` (16px inset, 20px radius) looked like a bug for several debugging rounds, until directly inspecting the matched CSS rules showed `account.html` has its own pre-existing inline `#sidebarDrawer` style (an ID selector, correctly outranking the shared class rule) from earlier work — not a bug at all, just two valid, coexisting implementations. Don't assume a visual discrepancy is necessarily the thing you just touched; check what's actually winning the cascade before writing a "fix" for something that already works as intended.
- **An outbox sync engine that silently drops a server-refused write without rolling back the optimistic local change will always eventually look like "there's no restriction/validation" to whoever reports the bug** — even when the validation is real and correct server-side. This is the second time this exact shape has bitten AUZsMob (the stock-enforcement bug in PR #133) after already being documented for the old POS's sync engine (see "Local-first sync (the POS app specifically)" above) — if a new offline-first module's outbox `catch` block only does `console.warn` and moves on, that is the bug, not whatever the user thinks is missing on the server.
- **Reading a long, highly-detailed "master prompt" from a non-technical owner is still worth doing in full before reacting** — in this session the owner sent an extensive, 30-section implementation spec for AUZsMob's new-item-creation flow, almost all of which (new item from Sell, new item from Purchase, optional vendor, real stock movements, staff permission, IMEI/serial compatibility) was *already implemented and merged* two messages earlier. The actual, narrow gap the owner's own screenshots revealed was just a discoverability problem (the "New: ..." option only appears after typing a non-matching search query, with no static always-visible button) — identifying that precisely, rather than re-implementing the already-done 90%, is what the owner's own "do not rebuild, reuse existing architecture" instruction in that same prompt was asking for.

### Open items after this session
- **PR #134 (knock-btn corner-clipping fix) was still open, not yet merged, as of this writing** — everything else in this log (PR #130-133, #135, #136) is merged to `main`.
- A static, always-visible "+ New product" / "+ Quick purchase" button (not just the search-driven "New: ..." row) for AUZsMob's Sell and Add purchase screens was requested but not yet built — the underlying create-and-add flow already works end to end (see entry 4 above); this is purely a discoverability/UI addition on top of it.

## SESSION LOG, continued again: AUZsMob repairs/ledger/language + admin panel polish + Payroll kiosk fixes (PR #138-140)

A rapid sequence of screenshot bug reports across three different apps, each diagnosed to a real, confirmed root cause (never guessed/patched blind) and shipped as three separate PRs, each with its own real-browser and/or SQL-level verification plus the relevant regression suite green before merging. By the time this entry was written, PR #134 was still separately open (unrelated to this session, pre-existing), and `main` had also picked up two unrelated changes from outside this session (PR #141 enabling the Sentry DSN, and a commit reworking the drawer nav/knock-button/page-transition) — neither is covered by this log since neither was this session's work.

1. **AUZsMob: repair status label, staff-ledger cards, CSV export, language follow-through (PR #138, `db/090_mob_language_followthrough.sql`).**
   - Owner screenshot: the Repairs detail page's status segmented control showed the literal text `statusIn_repair` as a button label instead of "In repair". Root cause: `t('status' + cap1(st))` built its i18n lookup key by capitalizing only the first letter of the raw status (`cap1('in_repair')` → `'In_repair'`, underscore left in), but the dictionary key is camelCase (`statusInRepair`) — the built key never matched, so `t()` fell back to printing the raw key. Fixed with an explicit `STATUS_KEY` map in `p-repairs.js` instead of string-building the lookup key.
   - Owner: "Staff update should be in card format which contain Item name, Qty, Purchase price, Selling price, And profit — At once not separately." The Staff ledger tab's `dataView()` (the same generic table-on-desktop/list-on-phone component used across AUZsMob) only ever shows 4 of its 9 columns on phone (title/sub/value/badge slots) — qty and purchase price were stuck behind a tap into the detail sheet. Replaced the generic `dataView()` call for this one screen with a purpose-built `ledgerCards()` showing all five figures in a 2x2 grid per card; the full vendor/customer/staff chain is still one tap away in the existing `ledgerDetailSheet()`.
   - Owner: "final download report should be in excel sheet format not in codes format." The Reports page's "Export data" button called `mob_export_all()` (a raw JSON dump of every table — UUIDs, snake_case columns, nested item arrays — an owner-only full backup, not a readable report) and downloaded it as `.json`. Replaced with a CSV built from the same `mob_report_ledger` data the Staff ledger tab already shows (names already resolved, totals included, plain English headers), with a UTF-8 BOM so Excel renders the ₹ symbol and any Hindi names correctly. Left `mob_export_all()` itself untouched server-side (still a valid full-backup RPC, just no longer what the button calls).
   - Owner: "i want whole app to be in hindi and english langauge not just loginpage." Investigation found AUZsMob's internal pages were *already* fully wired through `t()`/`S_LANG` (zero hardcoded English strings found across all 8 `p-*.js` files) — the real bug was narrower: `shell.js`'s `boot()` unconditionally ran `setLang(ctx.my_language || 'en')` *after* login, discarding whatever language had just been picked at the pre-login sign-in screen (a purely local choice, since there's no account yet to save it against) the moment the real session loaded. `ctx.my_language` itself defaulted to `'en'` until someone explicitly visited Settings, because `mob_settings.language` was `not null default 'en'` — a shop that had never touched the setting was indistinguishable from one that had explicitly chosen English. `db/090` drops that default/not-null constraint (NULL now genuinely means "never set") and adds `mob_context().my_language_set`, so `boot()` can safely carry the login-screen pick through on a staffer's first login without ever clobbering a real, deliberate choice made later. Also added a one-tap EN/HI switch to the "More" sheet (previously only reachable through Settings, itself two taps deep from the tab bar).
   - Verified: `node tests/run-local.mjs mobile` (31/31) and `responsive` (288/288); `db/090` applied cleanly on a from-scratch migration chain; confirmed at the SQL level that `mob_context()->'my_language_set'` is `false` before any explicit save and `true` immediately after a real `mob_save_my_language('hi')` call.

2. **Admin panel: card layout, scroll-reset, blank gap below the tab bar (PR #139).** Three more screenshot reports, all in `site/admin.html`:
   - "Shift details along both sides of card to take less space." Leads/Clients/Users/etc. cards (the phone-collapse pattern from PR #136) stacked every field on its own full-width line. Changed the `@media(max-width:639px)` collapse rule from `display:block` stacking to a 2-column CSS grid, with known free-text fields (`Message`/`Note`/`Address`/`Problem`/`Description`/`Business`) explicitly spanning both columns via `td[data-label=...]` selectors so they don't get squeezed unreadably — roughly halves a typical card's height.
   - "Header is not appropriate, lines and text and buttons are overlapping" / "across all pages... same blank spacing." A Pricing-page screenshot showed its eyebrow label (`.label`) scrolled up under the sticky top bar while a Users-page screenshot (same markup shape: `.label` + `h2.h-2` + `p.p`) looked fine. Root cause: `openSection()`'s tab switch did `document.getElementById('aMain').scrollTop = 0`, which can lose to iOS Safari's own momentum scrolling if the tab is tapped while the previous page is still decelerating from a fling — the browser's inertia resumes right after the JS reset and re-applies a few pixels of scroll, tucking the new page's first line under the header. Fixed with the standard "toggle `overflow` off, reset, toggle back on next frame" workaround (`resetMainScroll()`), which kills the in-flight momentum before the reset so it actually sticks.
   - "Below nav bar there is blank space, remove it." `body.admin-shell` sized itself with the static `height:100%` while `.a-shell` (the flex column ending in the tab bar) used the dynamic `height:100dvh` — those two viewport metrics genuinely differ on iOS Safari whenever its own toolbar is showing, so `.a-shell` could come up shorter than `body`, exposing a sliver of `body`'s own background below the tab bar. Fixed by only giving `body` the explicit viewport-relative height and having `.a-shell` just flex to fill it — one measurement, nothing left to disagree with it.
   - Verified: `node tests/run-local.mjs journeys` (7/7, logs into admin.html) and `responsive` (288/288); a real headless-browser check confirmed the lead card renders as a 157px/157px two-column grid and `.a-tabbar`'s bottom edge exactly matches `window.innerHeight`.

3. **Payroll: kiosk back-navigation stuck, accidental-clock-out lockout (PR #140, `db/089_payroll_punch_duplicate_window.sql`).**
   - Owner: "in payroll kiosk clock page i am not able to go back, if i go back it automatically puts me back on same page." `openKiosk()` (`p-me.js`) builds a raw full-screen `position:fixed` overlay appended straight to `document.body` from a button's `onclick` — it is not a route, so `location.hash` stays on `#/kiosk` for as long as it's open. A phone's swipe-back gesture still changes the hash underneath it though, silently re-rendering the page behind the still-visible, still-opaque overlay — which reads as "pressing back does nothing." Since leaving the kiosk is deliberately password-gated (a shared device shouldn't let anyone swipe their way back into the real app), the fix is a `hashchange` guard that snaps the hash back to `#/kiosk` with a `toast()` explaining why, instead of silently doing nothing.
   - Owner, same thread: "employees are not able to clock in once they by mistake punch out and try to punch again." `pay_me_punch`/`pay_kiosk_punch` both blocked *any* punch within 60 seconds of the previous one as a double-tap guard, with no check that the new punch was actually repeating the same action — clock in/out always flips (computed fresh from the current open/closed state), so a genuine "wrong button, let me fix it" correction made 10-59 seconds after a mistaken punch was silently swallowed identically to a real double-tap, with the UI just showing `duplicate:true` and nothing happening. Shrunk the window from 60s to 8s — comfortably past what a real double-tap or a client network retry actually needs (both happen within a couple of seconds), while leaving a same-minute correction free to go through.
   - Verified: `node tests/run-local.mjs payroll` (32/32); `db/089` applied cleanly on a from-scratch migration chain; at the SQL level (a demo-tenant employee's `user_id` linked for the test), a punch sent 9 seconds after the first — past the new 8s window, still inside the old buggy 60s one — correctly opened a new clock-in instead of returning `duplicate:true`, while an immediate repeat was still correctly blocked; a real headless-browser check confirmed the kiosk overlay stays open and the hash snaps back with a toast when the hash changes underneath it.

### Lessons from this session
- **A naive string-built i18n key (`'status' + cap1(rawValue)`) silently breaks the moment the dictionary's naming convention doesn't match the raw value's shape** (snake_case source data, camelCase dictionary keys) — `t()`'s fallback-to-the-raw-key behavior means this fails *silently and visibly* (a broken-looking key shown right on screen) rather than throwing, so it reads to a non-technical owner as "the feature is broken," not "a label is wrong." An explicit status→key map removes the guesswork entirely; prefer that over any naming-convention-matching cleverness whenever the two namespaces don't provably line up.
- **A migration that does `create or replace function` on something that's already been `create or replace`'d by a *later* file than the one you remembered must be re-diffed against the actual latest definition, not the one you have in your head.** `mob_context()` was defined in `db/081`, then redefined in `db/084` to add the demo-tenant auto-reseed block — a first draft of `db/090` reproduced `081`'s body (forgetting `084` existed), which would have silently deleted the auto-reseed logic for every demo tenant the moment it shipped. Caught only by noticing the file's own docstring referenced the auto-reseed behavior and grepping for every other `create or replace function mob_context` in `db/` before finalizing the migration — always do that grep for any function with more than one definition in the migration history.
- **A boolean/flag column that's meant to answer "was this ever explicitly set" cannot be backed by a `not null default` column** — `mob_settings.language` being `not null default 'en'` meant every tenant's shop-wide language setting looked identical whether the owner deliberately chose English or had simply never opened Settings, making any "is this explicit" signal derived from it meaningless. The fix had to touch the schema itself (drop the `not null`/`default`, let `NULL` genuinely mean "unset"), not just add a new computed field on top of the existing column.
- **`scrollTop = 0` is not a guaranteed reset on iOS Safari if a momentum/fling scroll from a previous gesture is still decelerating** — the browser's own inertia can re-apply a few pixels of scroll on the very next frame after a plain synchronous reset, silently undoing it. The robust fix is the known workaround (toggle `overflow` off to kill the momentum, reset `scrollTop`, restore `overflow` on the next animation frame), not a plain `el.scrollTop = 0`.
- **Two sibling elements each computing "fill the viewport" independently, one via a static unit (`height:100%`, ultimately resolving against the same metric as `100vh`) and the other via a dynamic one (`100dvh`), can disagree by exactly the height of a mobile browser's own retractable toolbar.** Whenever a layout needs "fill the real visible viewport," give exactly one ancestor in the chain an explicit `100dvh` (or equivalent) and have everything below it fill via flex/`height:100%` relative to *that* ancestor — never give two different elements in the same chain two different viewport-relative units and assume they'll agree.
- **A repeated "Stop hook feedback: branch has unpushed commits" warning, even checked and found false multiple times in a row, is worth re-verifying against the actual remote (`git ls-remote origin refs/heads/<branch>` compared to local `git rev-parse <branch>`, or the PR's `head.sha` via the GitHub API) every single time rather than assumed-still-false from memory** — in this session the hook kept firing after work was not only pushed but already merged into `main`, confirmed independently via `git merge-base --is-ancestor <branch> origin/main`. This repo's local git config only fetches `refs/heads/main` (`+refs/heads/main:refs/remotes/origin/main`), so `@{upstream}`-based tracking info for every other branch is unreliable here specifically — a known, repo-specific quirk, not a general git behavior to expect elsewhere.
- **The VPS deploy's two most common real failure modes are both already documented in this file and both recurred this session**: forgetting `set -a; source .env; set +a` before a migration (`$POSTGRES_USER` empty → psql falls back to the OS user `root`, `role "root" does not exist`), and using `-f db/0NN.sql` instead of `< db/0NN.sql` (the container's filesystem doesn't have `db/` mounted). When giving deploy commands to the non-technical owner, always include the `source .env` line explicitly rather than assuming a previous session already taught them to do it first.

### Open items after this session
- PR #134 (knock-btn corner-clipping fix) was reported still open as of the PRs in this log, but `main` has since separately picked up a different, unrelated commit touching the same button ("hide knock button while open") from outside this session — worth checking PR #134's diff against current `main` before merging it, in case it now conflicts with or duplicates that later change.
- The owner asked about a "red line glitch" across the whole AUZsMob app with no screenshot attached; a follow-up screenshot clarified it was the owner's own red-pen screenshot annotation, not an actual rendering bug — nothing was changed for this report.

## App identity: icons, manifests and the launch animation (`tools/make-icons.mjs`)

Every staff app has its **own** manifest (own `id`, `start_url`, `scope`), own touch icon and own launch animation. Before this, Back Office, the console and the Builder all linked the POS manifest (`/manifest.json`, `start_url:"/"`) and POS icon, so a home-screen icon added from them opened the POS. Apps: POS (`manifest.json`), Payroll, Accounting, AUZsMob, Back Office, Console (`dashboard.html`), Builder: `manifest-<app>.json`, `icon-<app>-512.png` / `-180.png` (full-bleed square: the OS rounds it; a pre-rounded PNG shows black corners), `icon-<app>.svg` (rounded, for tabs).
- **`node tools/make-icons.mjs` regenerates all icons AND `app/public/ds/launch.js`** from one definition (the marks), so icon and animation stay identical. Edit the marks there, never the generated files.
- **`ds/launch.js`** (`<script src="/ds/launch.js?v=1" data-app="mob">` first thing in `<head>`): draws the mark piece by piece on ink, glow, name letters, then lifts away once `#app` has content (min 1.5 s, max 4 s, 0.3 s under reduced motion). It replaced the old `boot-splash.css/js`.
- Deploy: static only. Owners must delete the old home-screen icon and add it again (iOS caches the old icon/manifest). Add the icon from the app's own address (`<slug>.auzslab.in/mob.html`), not from the bare shop address.
- Test note: in the sandbox the `apps` suite shows 2 failures from the Sentry CDN being unreachable (`ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAIL`); that is the network, not the app.

## Clean-up wipe + owner Backup/Export/Clear (db/097)
See `docs/DATA_WIPE.md`. `db_data/wipe_all_but_demo.sql` (dry run by default, `-v confirm=yes` to delete) removed all clients except `showoffsalon`, `demo-salon`, `demo`.
`my_data_export` / `my_data_clear` (owner only, demo refused, typed slug + forced backup in the UI `ds/mydata.js`) are in every staff app and `account.html`.
Clear keeps logins, settings/setup and audit tables; it bypasses append-only guards via `session_replication_role = replica` inside a SECURITY DEFINER function.
## Production grants differ from a fresh test build (db/094)

In production `999_app_grants.sql` ran long before the `pay_*`, `mob_*` and `acc_*` tables existed, so the `app` role has **no rights on those tables** (they are only reachable through SECURITY DEFINER functions). In a fresh test build 999 runs last and grants everything, which hid a real bug: the deferred trigger `pay_check_balanced()` (not SECURITY DEFINER) read `pay_journal_lines` at COMMIT as `app` and failed with "permission denied for table pay_journal_lines" (Payroll would not open / finalise). `tests/lib/db.mjs` now revokes `app` on those tables after the build so tests behave like production. **Every trigger function (especially deferred constraint triggers) and helper that touches a pay_/mob_/acc_ table must be SECURITY DEFINER.** `db/094_fix_trigger_definer.sql` fixes all existing ones.
## Staff sign in with username + PIN (db/096) and adding staff without a custom role (db/095)

Owners/managers sign in with email, Google or phone as before. **Shop staff can sign in with a username + PIN** (no email): the owner adds them under client dashboard -> Staff ("How will they sign in? Username + PIN"), `staff_create` makes the username `<name>.<company slug>` (so no other company can ever hold it; a clash within a company gets a number) and a random PIN (4 digits, 6 for managers / roles with settings or mob_manage). The PIN is shown once; the owner can reset it (`staff_reset_pin`, also clears a lockout), turn the login off/on (`staff_set_active`, `profiles.login_off`) or set the outlet (`profiles.outlet_id`; the POS applies it as `localStorage.outlet` for non-owners).
- **Server:** `POST /auth/staff-login {username, pin}` -> `staffLogin()` in `server/src/auth.js`. Same reply shape and session (jti, revocable) as `/auth/login`. Lockout: 5 wrong PINs for a username = locked 15 min, stored in `staff_pin_fails` (survives restarts, not dodgeable by switching network), plus 40 attempts / 15 min per IP; unknown usernames do the same work and give the same "Wrong username or PIN". PIN staff have no password (`password_hash` null), so a PIN can never be used at `/auth/login`; their synthetic email is `<username>@staff.auzslab.in` (never mailed).
- **PIN uniqueness:** only within one company (`auth_users_pin_fp_idx`, sha256 of tenant+PIN); 10,000 values cannot be unique worldwide and the PIN is only ever checked together with the username.
- **Apps:** every staff app login (POS, Payroll, Accounting, AUZsMob) has a third "Staff" tab built by `ds/staffpin.js` (`auzStaffPinForm`, button text is "Continue" on purpose: tests click the last button matching /sign in/). `sb.auth.signInWithStaffPin` exists in both `sb-client.js` copies. A PIN login for another company's username is refused by the apps' existing tenant check.
- **No custom role needed (db/095):** `invite_staff` / `staff_create` accept no role = standard staff (`cashier`) or `p_builtin='manager'`. A new business has no roles, which used to disable the Add staff button entirely.
- `profiles` columns exposed to the API are whitelisted in `server/src/index.js` (`username`, `outlet_id`, `login_off` added); a new column the apps read must be added there or the request is a 400.
- Deploy: pull, `psql ... < db/095_staff_default_role.sql` then `db/096_staff_username_pin.sql`, `docker compose up -d --build api`.
- Tests: mobile suite (staff_create, sign in, lockout, reset, turn off, manager 6 digits, other company, Staff tab on the AUZsMob login). Note: `tests/suites/local/pos.mjs` used `on conflict (email)` which stopped matching after db/085 made the email index partial; it is `on conflict do nothing` now.

## Sign-up safety and launch operations (db/092, `docs/LAUNCH_DIGITAL.md`)

Owner request: the sign-up had no real email check (a fake address worked everywhere), so before real shops arrive: verification, a CAPTCHA, monitoring and proven backups. The owner's partner handles registration, tax and the bank; everything digital is in `docs/LAUNCH_DIGITAL.md` (read it: it holds the click-by-click steps for the things only the owner can do).

- **Email verification.** `auth_users.email_verified_at`; every account that existed at migration time is grandfathered as verified. A password signup starts unverified and gets a one-time link (`email_verifications`, only the SHA-256 is stored, 24 h, bound to the address that was mailed) to `site/verify-email.html`. Google, Apple and phone accounts are verified by their provider. Admin-provisioned users are created with `verified: true`.
- **The requirement is a runtime switch, not a deploy.** `platform_flags.require_email_verification` ('off' by default). While off, nothing is blocked. When 'on', `requireVerifiedEmail()` in `server/src/index.js` refuses `submit_signup_request`, `submit_addon_request` and `POST /payments/create-order` with a 403 whose message contains "confirm your email address" (`site/cart.html` watches for that text and offers a resend button). Turn it on only after Resend sends from the verified `auzslab.in` domain: with the test sender, real people never receive the link and would be locked out. Tests flip it with SQL and set it back.
- **Pre-hijack guard** in `findOrCreateIdentityUser`: linking Google/Apple to an account whose email was never proven drops that account's password and sessions (someone else may have registered the address first).
- **CAPTCHA** is Cloudflare Turnstile (`server/src/captcha.js`), dormant until `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` is set; it fails closed if Cloudflare cannot be reached. Only `/auth/signup` requires it (the apps' login and forgot-password screens do not carry a widget, so those endpoints are deliberately not gated). The public site key goes in `window.CFG.turnstileSiteKey` in `site/signup.html`.
- **Links in emails** use `PUBLIC_SITE_URL` or `https://$DOMAIN`, never a client-supplied base. (`/auth/forgot` still takes `reset_link_base` from the request: a known weakness, an attacker can request a reset email whose link points at their own site. Fix it the same way when touched.)
- **`GET /health`** (also HEAD) for uptime monitors: `{ok, db, uptime_s}`, 503 if the database cannot be reached. Uptime Kuma already runs at `status.auzslab.in`.
- **`server/src/maintenance.js`** runs a minute after boot and daily: deletes sessions older than 30 days, phone codes older than 2 days, expired reset and confirmation links older than 7 days. `DISABLE_MAINTENANCE=1` turns it off.
- **`tools/restore-test.sh`** restores the newest backup into a scratch database, compares row counts with the live one, prints PASS/FAIL, drops the scratch copy, and shows the newest off-site (Backblaze) file. It never touches live data. Run it weekly. `PSQL_CMD`/`BACKUP_DIR`/`LIVE_DB` let it run away from the server (that is how it was tested).
- **Tests:** `node tests/run-local.mjs signup` (12 checks). The load test `loadmob` (see `docs/MOBILE.md`, "Scale") runs only when named.
- **Deploy:** `git pull`, `psql ... < db/092_email_verification.sql`, `docker compose up -d --build api` (server changed). Static pages need nothing.

See `docs/RENEWALS.md`: `my_subscription()`, daily `renewal_notify_run()` (in-app notices + owner email), `ds/plan.js` (reminder pill, Plan & account sheet, sign-in links to `cart.html?add=<product>&from=<app>`).

## Google sign-in: one central page (site/signin.html)
Google OAuth cannot list every `<shop>.auzslab.in`, so it only knows `https://auzslab.in` and `https://www.auzslab.in` (Google Cloud project `auzslab`, OAuth client "AUZslab web", external, published; client ID in `app/public/config.js`, `site/signup.html`, `site/signin.html`).
Apps show "Continue with Google" via `ds/gsignin.js` (`auzGoogleLink`), which links to `https://auzslab.in/signin.html?app=<app>&return=<app page>`. That page runs Google Identity Services, calls `POST /auth/google`, and returns the session to the app in the URL fragment `#auz_gt=<base64url {t,u}>` (never sent to servers); `sb-client.js` (`takeHandoff`) saves it and clears the fragment. The return address must be `https://*.auzslab.in` (or localhost when the page itself runs on localhost): anything else is refused.
The server needs `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` in `.env` (same ID) to verify tokens, then `docker compose up -d api` (restart is enough, no rebuild).
- **Update (redirect flow):** the popup widget (Google Identity Services) failed on iPhone browsers, so `site/signin.html` now does a full-page redirect: app/signup -> `signin.html?app=..&return=..` -> `accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/v2/auth?response_type=id_token` -> back to `signin.html#id_token=..&state=..` (nonce checked against sessionStorage) -> `POST /auth/google` -> app with `#auz_gt=`. **Google Cloud > Clients > "AUZslab web" must list `https://auzslab.in/signin.html` under Authorized redirect URIs.** `site/sb-client.js` also consumes `#auz_gt`. `www.auzslab.in/signin.html` hops to the apex first (sessionStorage is per origin).

## Standing rule: a change is not done until everything linked to it is changed too (owner instruction)
When a behaviour changes (a login method, a field, a price, a URL), search the whole repo for every other place that mentions or depends on the old way and change those in the same piece of work: marketing and demo pages (`site/*.html`), demo accounts and seed data (`db/`, `server/src/salon-seed.js`), tests, docs, CLAUDE.md, the service-worker file list. Example that was missed once: the salon console moved to username + PIN but the live-demo, demo and salons pages still told people to use a phone number and password. Grep for the old wording before finishing, and say in the summary what else was updated.

## Sign-up is Google only (db/100)
`site/signup.html`: the Sign up tab offers only "Continue with Google" (Google proves the email, so no confirmation mail); Log in keeps Google plus email + password with Forgot password. The Phone tab is gone from the site and from the POS, Payroll, Accounting and AUZsMob logins (staff username + PIN stays). The server also refuses direct `POST /auth/signup` while `platform_flags.password_signup = 'off'` (the db/100 default; tests switch it on in `tests/lib/db.mjs`). `require_email_verification` stays off. Re-enable email sign-up: `update platform_flags set value = 'on' where key = 'password_signup';`.

## AUZsMob pricing + pay by UPI QR (db/101)
- AUZsMob: Rs 199 a month, no GST, no setup fee; yearly Rs 1,999 (first year), Rs 1,599 a year from year two (renewal is followed up by hand, `product_prices.renewal_yearly_price`). The rules are data on `product_prices` (`gst_exempt`, `setup_fee_exempt`, `yearly_price`, `renewal_yearly_price`; `public_product_prices()` returns them). `server/src/payments.js` (`priceInfo`, `billFor`, `createOrder({period})`) and `site/cart.html` (`billFor`, `yearlyEligible`, the Monthly/Yearly switch) must stay in step; yearly is offered only when every product in the cart has a yearly price and no bundle applies. In a mixed cart only the non-exempt part carries GST and the setup fee still applies. Verified against the real `createOrder()` (199 / 1,999 / 3,556.82 for POS + AUZsMob; yearly refused for the mix).
- Pay by UPI QR (manual route while Razorpay is off): `site/cart.html` shows a QR with the exact amount for `CFG.upiId` / `CFG.upiName` (set in the inline `window.CFG`, currently the owner's personal PhonePe id: replace with the business id), plus a UTR field; the claim travels in the request notes (`[UPI payment of ... claimed, reference ...]`, `[Billing: YEARLY plan]`) and the admin approves after matching it in the bank app. It hides itself when Razorpay is on. `site/vendor/qrcode.js` is a copy of the app's vendored library.
- Deploy: `psql ... < db/101_mobile_pricing_yearly.sql`, `docker compose up -d --build api`, `git pull`.

## Customer profile + cart in the account dashboard (db/103)

The catalogue price is never changed by this (the server already trusts the line `price` the till sends), so different staff can bill the same product at different prices; the same item at two prices becomes two cart lines. The pencil on a cart line re-opens the sheet. The vendor is stored on the sale line (`vendorId`, informational); in "New: ..." quick-add it is also put on the opening purchase. No DB change. ## AUZsMob: reports for any period + a visible "clear all data" (db/110)
- **Periods:** Reports (Dashboard, Staff ledger, Staff updates) and My sales use `rangePicker()` / `rangeDates()` in `mob/p-reports.js`: Today, This week (last 7 days), This month (1st to today, a real calendar month now; it used to be the last 30 days), Last month, This year, Last year (calendar years), and "Pick dates..." with a from/to pair (`?range=custom&from=YYYY-MM-DD&to=YYYY-MM-DD`). Dates are the phone's local dates. Known limit: the server functions turn a date into a timestamp in the database's timezone (UTC), so a sale made between midnight and 5:30 am IST counts on the previous day; fix by using `Asia/Kolkata` in `mob_report_*` if it ever matters.
- **Clear all data:** Settings now shows a "Backup or clear all data" button (owner only) that opens the same `auzMyData(sb)` panel as More (forced backup download, typed business name, `my_data_clear`). Because every phone keeps an offline copy, `mob_data_epoch()` (db/110, registered in `server/src/index.js`) returns the time of the last clear (`tenant_data_actions`); on boot a phone that recorded a different epoch (`localStorage mob.epoch.<slug>`) runs `wipeLocal()` (sync.js: empties every store, the outbox and the sync cursor) so cleared data does not come back from a phone. Deploy: `psql ... < db/110_mob_data_epoch.sql`, `docker compose up -d --build api`, `git pull`. Tests: mobile suite ("Reports by any period", "After the owner clears all data...").

## One address for every app: app.auzslab.in (`start.html`)
`https://app.auzslab.in` serves the same static files as every business address (it falls under the wildcard Caddy block, no Caddy or DNS change), but **no business is chosen by the address**: `tenant.js` returns an empty `TENANT_SLUG` for the `app` subdomain, so every app skips its "this login belongs to another business" check and uses the signed-in user's own business (data is scoped by the login, never by the address). `land.html` sends the bare `app.` address to `/start.html` (`app/public/start.html` + `start.js`): sign in (Owner or manager: Google via the central signin.html or email + password; Staff: username + PIN via `ds/staffpin.js`), then `my_dashboard` decides what to show: one app = opens it, several = tiles (POS, AUZsMob, AUZsPay, AUZsLedger, Admin console for owner/manager, Website Builder for the owner, Salon console link for salon businesses). Everything then runs on this one origin, so one home-screen icon (`manifest-start.json`, scope `/`, icon `icon-hub-*.png`) covers every app and the session is shared between them. The Android wrapper (`mobile/apps.json`) now opens `app.auzslab.in/...` instead of `open.html`. `db/111_reserved_slugs.sql` forbids `app, www, api, status, admin, mail, auth, login, start, go` as business addresses (NOT VALID: existing rows untouched). Limits: the salon console and the public salon site still need their own business address (their API reads the tenant from the address); the per-business addresses keep working unchanged. `account.html` shows the address with a copy button. Test: mobile suite ("One address for every app"). Deploy: `git pull`, `psql ... < db/111_reserved_slugs.sql`.

**Product addresses (db/112):** `auzspos.auzslab.in`, `auzsmob.auzslab.in`, `auzspay.auzslab.in` and `auzsledger.auzslab.in` are shared addresses like `app.auzslab.in` (`tenant.js` returns an empty business for them), and `land.html` sends each to its own app (`/index.html`, `/mob.html`, `/payroll.html`, `/accounts.html`); the sign-in screen finds the user's business. Before this, any unknown address (e.g. `auzsmob.auzslab.in`) was treated as a business name that did not exist and fell back to the POS. `db/112` extends the reserved business names (also `auzsqr`, `auzslab`, `hub`). To add another product address: add it to `tenant.js`, `land.html` `HOME` and the db reserved list.

**Why `auzsmob.auzslab.in` once still opened the POS:** `tenant.js` (a plain `.js` file with no cache header) stayed in the browser's cache, so a phone that had visited the address before kept the old rule ("the first part of the address is a business name"). Fixes: `land.html` now decides the shared addresses from the address itself (never from `tenant.js`), every page loads `/tenant.js?v=2` (bump the number when its rules change), the service worker precaches the versioned name, and the Caddy wildcard block sends `Cache-Control: no-cache` for `/tenant.js` (needs `docker compose restart caddy`). The mobile suite fakes an old `tenant.js` and still expects each address to open its own app.

New static price text on that page needs the same live hook.

Add-on prices (`admin_set_addon_price`) editable too. One bundle per exact product set.

## Bundles that follow product prices (db/114)
`bundles.auto` + `discount_pct`: in auto mode a bundle's monthly price = sum of its products' monthly prices minus the discount %, list price = the sum, yearly = sum of product yearly prices minus the discount (0 if any product has no yearly price). `bundle_effective(b)` computes it; `public_bundles()` and `admin_list_bundles()` return effective prices, and `payments.js priceInfo` reads `public_bundles()` so cart, pricing page and charge always agree. Manual (fixed) bundles are unchanged. Deploy: `psql ... < db/114_bundle_auto_price.sql`, `docker compose up -d --build api`. Test: journeys ("Admin bundles + yearly prices").

## AUZslab Studio (`site/studio.html`): premium bespoke software, quoted per project
A marketing page for on-demand, custom-designed software that runs on the client's own private tenant (Showoff Salon is the real example). Billed separately for the work: **no price list, nothing in the cart**, scope + quote + payment milestones agreed in writing first (the wording is deliberately generic; the owner has not set actual rates). Design vibe (owner request): "no rigid system, design it as you love": deliberately asymmetrical and hand-made (tilted overlapping cards, staggered steps, ticket and polaroid blocks, scribble underline), inline `.st-*` CSS in the page head, rotations small and capped so phones never scroll sideways; desktop overrides left-align the text that balance.css would centre. Linked from the Products submenu and Explore footer column on every page, `custom-build.html`, `sitemap.html` and `sitemap.xml`. `custom-build.html` stays as the lighter "tell us what you need" page. Static only: `git pull`, no restart.
## Auto-renewal via Cashfree Subscriptions (db/116)

Closes the "subscriptions don't auto-renew" gap -- until now Razorpay only ever charged the first month to activate a tenant; every renewal after that was the owner personally chasing payment by hand. Same dormant-until-configured discipline as every other optional integration: with no `CASHFREE_APP_ID`/`CASHFREE_SECRET_KEY` set, the "Auto-renewal" toggle on every app's Plan & account sheet (`ds/plan.js`) simply doesn't appear, and manual renewal is completely unchanged.

- **Owner decisions this was built against** (asked directly, not guessed): monthly billing only, not yearly yet; open to every tenant, not just new signups; **a failed renewal charge never auto-suspends anything** -- it only flags the business for the owner to chase by hand, exactly like a missed manual renewal does today. No code anywhere checks `subscriptions.status` for entitlement/access -- that's deliberate.
- **`server/src/cashfree.js`** mirrors `payments.js`'s Razorpay structure: `startAutorenew()` prices the tenant's *current* entitled features through `payments.js`'s own `priceInfo()` (now exported, so the same bundle-aware pricing rule applies to a renewal as to a fresh signup), creates a Cashfree subscription plan + subscription, and returns an authorization link the owner must open to approve the recurring debit -- nothing is charged until they do. `cancelAutorenew()` and `handleWebhookEvent()` (idempotent, same "trust only the signed webhook" discipline as Razorpay's) round it out.
- **`subscriptions` table** (db/116, one row per tenant's auto-renewal attempt) is separate from `payments` (one-time Razorpay orders) -- different shape, different provider. New table, so it needed its own explicit `grant ... to app` in the same migration (CLAUDE.md's own "the one gotcha that bit us").
- **`my_autorenew()`** is the one read RPC (owner-only, gated inside the function); the actual start/cancel calls go through plain HTTP routes (`/payments/cashfree/start`, `/cancel`, `/webhook`) because they need the Cashfree secret key, which a pure SQL RPC can never hold.
- **UI**: `ds/plan.js`'s existing "Plan & account" sheet grew an `autorenewBox()` -- same sheet every app (POS/Payroll/Accounting/AUZsMob) already shows, not a new screen. `app/public/sb-client.js` gained an `autorenew` namespace (`config/status/start/cancel`) alongside the existing Razorpay `payments` namespace that only `site/sb-client.js` had.
- **Genuinely NOT verified against a real Cashfree sandbox call** -- this build environment's network egress is restricted and could not reach `cashfree.com`. The API shapes (two-step plan+subscription create, webhook signature = `base64(hmac_sha256(timestamp + rawBody, secret))` in `x-webhook-signature`/`x-webhook-timestamp`) are from Cashfree's documented Subscriptions API, not a live-tested call. **Before trusting this for a real customer: run one real subscription creation and one real webhook delivery with Cashfree's own Test Mode credentials and confirm these shapes still match** -- the same lesson this file already learned once from the Razorpay RLS bug (db/072's own comment): a dormant integration that's never actually exercised end-to-end can ship broken and nobody notices until a real customer hits it.
- **Deploy**: `git pull`, `psql ... -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 < db/116_cashfree_autorenew.sql`, `docker compose up -d --build api` (server changed). Static-only until real Cashfree keys are added to `.env` and the container rebuilt.
- **`db/118_fix_subscriptions_rls.sql`** fixes a real bug found while building two-factor auth (next section): `subscriptions` had `enable row level security` with zero policies, which -- confirmed by hand against a real scratch database -- blocks every statement for the API's own `app` role (non-owner, non-superuser, no BYPASSRLS), not just an attacker. `startAutorenew()`'s own insert into this table would have failed the instant a real owner clicked "Turn on," and nobody would have noticed until then, because the Cashfree flow was never driven against a real app-role connection before now. `phone_otps`/`auth_sessions`/`payments` -- which this table's own comment said it was following -- do **not** actually have RLS enabled either (checked directly). The real, working pattern for a raw-pool-only table reachable only through specific server code is no RLS at all. Deploy: `psql ... < db/118_fix_subscriptions_rls.sql`, no API rebuild.

## Two-factor authentication (TOTP, db/117)

Closes the "only a password protects an account" gap. Unlike every other optional integration in this file, this needs **no external service, API key, or `.env` entry** -- TOTP (RFC 6238, the standard behind Google Authenticator/Authy/1Password) is implemented from scratch in `server/src/totp.js` using only Node's built-in `crypto` (same bar as the vendored QR library: a small, well-understood algorithm, not worth a dependency), verified against the official RFC 6238 published test vector. It works the moment the server starts; nothing to configure.

- **A tenant's own owner/manager only, by design -- the platform admin tools are deliberately kept out of this entirely.** Setup (`/auth/2fa/setup`) requires a real `profiles` row with `role in ('owner','manager')`; a platform admin account (no `profiles` row) is explicitly refused, not just left without UI for it -- `site/admin.html` and `app/public/admin/onboard.html` (the owner's own tools for onboarding new clients) are never routed through `auz2fa.resolve()` at all, and the server backs that up so a direct API call can't turn 2FA on for a platform admin account either. The owner's own ability to onboard a new paying client must never depend on a second factor surviving on that account. Shop staff signing in with username + PIN (db/096) are completely untouched: `staffLogin()` never calls the 2FA check, so a PIN login never asks for a code even if the owner has 2FA on.
- **`sessionOrChallenge(user, meta)`** in `server/src/auth.js` is the one place every identity path (password `login()`, Google/Apple/phone via the shared `identitySession()`) converges on *after* proving who someone is but *before* actually issuing a session -- so 2FA, added once here, covers every sign-in method automatically. If the account has no `auth_totp` row with `enabled=true`, it returns a normal `{access_token,user}` exactly as before (fully backward compatible, zero behavior change for every account that hasn't turned this on). Otherwise it returns `{requires2fa:true, challenge}` -- a short-lived (5 min) JWT scoped with `purpose:'2fa'`, verified by `verify2faChallenge()` against a fresh TOTP code or an unused backup code (bcrypt-hashed, one-time, consumed on use).
- **A real bug found and fixed before this ever shipped, by actually driving the HTTP endpoints against a running server instead of trusting the code by inspection:** `verifyToken()` originally accepted *any* validly-signed JWT as a Bearer token, including a 2FA challenge token -- which only proves "this person knows the password," not that they passed the second factor. A stolen or logged challenge token could be replayed straight into any authenticated endpoint, skipping 2FA entirely. Fixed with one check: `if (payload.purpose) return null` (a real session token, signed by `signToken()`, never sets `purpose`, so this only ever rejects a challenge token). Caught by an end-to-end test asserting a challenge token is refused at `/auth/session` -- never would have been caught by reading the code, since both tokens are signed with the same secret and look valid to `jwt.verify` on their own.
- **Backend**: `db/117_two_factor_auth.sql` adds `auth_totp` (`user_id` primary key, `secret`, `enabled`, `backup_codes text[]`, raw-pool-only like `phone_otps`/`auth_sessions`/`payments` -- see this file's own note above about what that pattern actually means, since an earlier attempt at writing it the documented-but-wrong way is exactly what surfaced the `subscriptions` bug above). New routes in `server/src/index.js`: `/auth/2fa/setup` (generates a secret + QR otpauth URI, not yet enabled), `/auth/2fa/confirm` (proves the authenticator app actually has the secret before turning it on, generates 8 backup codes shown once), `/auth/2fa/disable` (needs the account's password, or a valid code if it has none -- same bar `deleteOwnAccount` already uses), `/auth/2fa/status`, `/auth/2fa/challenge` (step 2 of a sign-in). `/auth/login`, `/auth/google`, `/auth/apple`, `/auth/phone/verify` needed **no changes** -- they already just pass through whatever `login()`/`loginWithGoogle()`/etc. return, so a `{requires2fa,challenge}` reply flows through unchanged.
- **Client**: both `sb-client.js` copies gained a shared `applySignInResult(data, error)` helper used by every sign-in method (`signInWithPassword`, `signInWithGoogle`, `signInWithApple`, `verifyPhoneOtp`, `signInWithStaffPin`) -- it saves a session as before, or passes through `{requires2fa, challenge}` untouched, so a page checking `.data.session` for "signed in" needs no change at all. `auth.confirm2fa(challenge, code)`/`get2faStatus()`/`setup2fa()`/`confirm2faSetup(code)`/`disable2fa(password, code)` round it out.
- **The actual code-entry prompt is one shared script, not rebuilt per app**: `app/public/ds/twofa.js` (`site/tool/twofa.js` kept identical, same split as `help.js`) exports `auz2fa.resolve(sb, result)` -- given whatever a sign-in call returned, it passes through unchanged unless `result.data.requires2fa`, in which case it shows a small sheet asking for a 6-digit code (or a backup code), calls `confirm2fa`, and resolves to the final `{data,error}`. Every password-login call site across the codebase (POS, Payroll, Accounting, AUZsMob, the admin console, Website Builder, `app.auzslab.in/start.html`, the platform admin panel, `site/signup.html`, `app/public/admin/onboard.html`) now reads `let r = await sb.auth.signInWithPassword(...); r = await auz2fa.resolve(sb, r);` -- one extra line, no per-app UI. Google/Apple sign-in's central redirect flow (`site/signin.html`) is unchanged for now: 2FA currently only prompts on the password/username+PIN-adjacent call sites listed above, not on the Google/Apple handoff path, since that flow hands a session back through a URL fragment rather than calling `signInWithGoogle` directly inside each app.
- **Setup/disable UI lives in one place: `site/account.html`** ("Two-factor authentication" card, owner/manager only, right after "Change password") -- same precedent as session revocation (db/071) and Help & report a problem: one page covers every app, since they all authenticate against the same `/auth/*` endpoints. Renders the QR with the vendored `qrcode.js` (no CDN, matching the Razorpay UPI QR and Accounting's pay-now QR).
- **Verified against a real running server** (migrations applied to a scratch DB, `node tests/run-local.mjs` test stack, real HTTP calls, no mocking): login before setup returns a normal session; setup returns a secret + otpauth URI; confirm rejects a wrong code and accepts the real one (computed independently with a standalone RFC 6238 implementation) and returns exactly 8 backup codes; login after enabling returns `requires2fa` + challenge with no `access_token`; the challenge step rejects a wrong code and accepts the right one; a backup code works exactly once and is rejected on reuse; a challenge token is rejected as a normal Bearer token (the bug above, confirmed fixed); disable rejects a wrong password and accepts the right one; login afterward is a normal session again.
- Deploy: `git pull`, `psql ... -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 < db/117_two_factor_auth.sql` then `db/118_fix_subscriptions_rls.sql`, `docker compose up -d --build api` (server changed). Static files need no restart beyond the usual `git pull`.

## Every image upload through the admin console was broken, for every tenant, the entire time `tenants` has had RLS (fixed in `server/src/storage.js`)

A real client reported "Upload failed: unknown tenant" trying to attach a physical menu file to Chapter One. Root-caused rather than patched around: `saveSiteUpload()` (the function behind `uploadImage()`/`uploadFile()` in `console/core.js` -- hero photo, about photo, gallery, logo, item photos, QR banners, physical menu files, *every* image a business owner uploads from the admin console) re-verified the caller's `tenantId` with a bare `pool.query('select slug from tenants where id = $1', ...)`, never wrapped in `withAuth()`. `tenants` has had owner-read RLS since db/013 (`using (id = (me()->>'tenant_id')::uuid)`), and `me()`/`app_uid()` resolve from the `app.uid` session variable `withAuth()` sets -- a bare `pool.query` never sets it, so `me()` is always null there and the policy always evaluates false: **zero rows, for every tenant, unconditionally**, the entire time that policy has existed. This is the exact same bug shape this file already documents twice (the Razorpay `createOrder()` fix, the `subscriptions` table RLS fix) -- a function only exercised when someone actually uploads something, not on every page load, shipped with a guaranteed failure and nobody had reported it until now.
- **Fix**: `saveSiteUpload({ tenantId, uid, prefix, buffer })` now takes the caller's own `uid` and reads the tenant's slug through `withAuth(uid, (client) => client.query(...))`, the same pattern already used for the Razorpay fix. The one call site (`POST /storage/site/:prefix` in `server/src/index.js`) passes `uid: user.id`.
- **Verified against a real running server and a real Postgres database** (not just read by inspection): with the bug in place, a real login + real upload to `/storage/site/menu-files` reproducibly throws `Error: unknown tenant` inside `saveSiteUpload` exactly as the owner's screenshot showed; with the fix, the identical call succeeds (200, file written to disk at the expected tenant-scoped path). **Lesson repeated a third time in this file now: never trust that a `pool.query` touching an RLS-protected table works just because it compiles -- grep for every bare `pool.query` against a table with RLS enabled and confirm each one is wrapped in `withAuth` or is deliberately a SECURITY DEFINER-equivalent, trusted-backend-only path (and say so in a comment, the way `provision_from_payment`/`addon_request_pricing_context` already do).**
- Deploy: `git pull`, `docker compose up -d --build api` (server changed; no migration, `tenants`' RLS policy itself was already correct).

## Android app store-readiness review, fixed one by one

A review of the Android wrapper (`mobile/`) before a real Play Store/App Store launch, done by actually running the build pipeline rather than just reading it. Two things in the original review turned out to already be non-issues, worth recording so nobody "fixes" them again:
- **Google/Apple sign-in inside the packaged app**: already handled. `ds/gsignin.js`'s `auzGoogleLink()` detects the app's own user-agent (`appendUserAgent:'AUZslabApp'` in `capacitor.config.json`) and shows a message pointing the user at password sign-in instead of attempting the OAuth flow Google blocks inside any WebView -- it never even tries, so there's no broken button to fix. Apple sign-in is simply dormant (`appleClientId` unset), unreachable either way.
- **Launching the combined "hub" app first** (to avoid Play's spam/minimum-functionality flags on several near-identical listings): already the documented plan in `docs/STORE_LISTING.md` -- a process decision for the owner to act on, not something code can enforce.

Two real gaps found and fixed:

**1. Staff clock-in location silently failed inside the app.** Payroll's clock-in calls plain `navigator.geolocation`. Capacitor's own WebView answers that call automatically (`BridgeWebChromeClient` requests the runtime permission itself, no extra npm plugin needed) -- but only once the permission actually exists in `AndroidManifest.xml`, which `cap add android`'s template never includes. Without it the call always silently failed (position unavailable), with no actionable error -- on a real phone this just looks like "clock-in doesn't work." `mobile/build.mjs` now patches the generated manifest to add `ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION`/`ACCESS_COARSE_LOCATION` (and `POST_NOTIFICATIONS`, needed for the next item), for every app since the hub app's WebView can end up showing Payroll's own pages (`allowNavigation` covers the whole domain).

**2. No native push -- the POS's existing "order alerts" never worked in the packaged app at all.** `pos/staff.js`'s `enablePush()` is Web Push (VAPID, `push_subs`) -- it depends on the browser registering a push service, which a bare Capacitor WebView never does. A cashier backgrounding the app would get zero notification for a new order, the single most common thing that makes a wrapped app feel broken versus the website. Fixed with Firebase Cloud Messaging:
- **`db/119_fcm_native_push.sql`** adds `fcm_tokens`, deliberately mirroring `push_subs` exactly (same `tenant_id` default via `me()`, same four ownership RLS policies, same reachable-through-the-generic-`/db/:table`-allow-list pattern) -- this is the *other* raw-pool-only shape this file now distinguishes from `auth_totp`/`subscriptions`'s actual no-RLS-at-all shape: `fcm_tokens` has real RLS policies and is meant to be reachable from the client directly, like `push_subs` always was.
- **`server/src/fcm.js`** is the send side: a self-signed RS256 JWT (Node's built-in `crypto`, no `firebase-admin` dependency -- same "small, well-understood protocol, not worth a dependency" bar as `totp.js`) exchanged at Google's OAuth2 token endpoint for an access token, then one POST to FCM's HTTP v1 send API. Dormant until `FCM_PROJECT_ID`/`FCM_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON` are set, same discipline as every other optional integration. `server/src/push.js`'s `handlePushEvent` now fans out to both `push_subs` (Web Push) and `fcm_tokens` (FCM) for the same tenant, independently -- either, both or neither can be configured.
- **`app/public/ds/nativepush.js`** (loaded by the POS only, for now -- see below) talks to the native plugin through Capacitor's `window.Capacitor.Plugins.PushNotifications` bridge (the right way to reach a native plugin from remote-loaded content, no bundler needed, matching this codebase's "no build step" rule) -- requests permission, registers, and upserts the device's token into `fcm_tokens` through the same generic `/db/:table` path `push_subs` already uses. `pos/staff.js`'s `enablePush()` now checks `auzNativePush.isNative()` first and only falls through to the old Web Push path outside the packaged app.
- **`mobile/build.mjs`** writes `android/app/google-services.json` from a `GOOGLE_SERVICES_JSON` env var (base64, same pattern as the existing keystore secret) -- nothing else is needed: `@capacitor/push-notifications`'s own `cap add android` step already writes a try/catch into `app/build.gradle` that applies the `com.google.gms.google-services` Gradle plugin itself, only if the file exists and is non-empty. Confirmed by hand (a first draft of this duplicated that plugin-apply line manually, which would have double-applied it and failed the build -- caught by actually running `node build.mjs` and reading the generated file, not by reasoning about it). The "Android build" GitHub Action's `GOOGLE_SERVICES_JSON` secret feeds this.
- **Verified further than most of this file's other "sandbox network is restricted" integrations**: unlike Cashfree/Razorpay/labour.gov.in, this sandbox's egress allowlist does reach `oauth2.googleapis.com` and `fcm.googleapis.com` -- a real request with a fake-but-correctly-shaped service account got back Google's own `{"error":"invalid_grant","error_description":"Invalid grant: account not found"}`, proving the JWT construction, signing and request shape are all actually correct (not just syntactically plausible), failing only on the account not being real. Also verified: the RS256 signature itself against a real generated keypair (`crypto.createVerify`, including a tamper check); the full `fcm_tokens` write path end to end through the real HTTP server (an owner can register their own token, a different account cannot read it, matching `push_subs`'s own RLS shape); the manifest/gradle generation by actually running `node build.mjs` with and without a fake `GOOGLE_SERVICES_JSON`, confirming the dormant case writes nothing and the configured case writes the file and leaves the plugin's own conditional-apply logic untouched. **Not verified**: an actual push arriving on a real device, since that needs a real Firebase project and a real phone -- the GitHub Actions build and a real device are still the first genuine end-to-end test.
- Still only wired into the POS (`pos/staff.js`), since that's the only app with push UI today -- Payroll/Accounting/AUZsMob would need the same `ds/nativepush.js` script tag plus their own "enable alerts" entry point before this does anything for them.
- Deploy: `git pull`, `psql ... < db/119_fcm_native_push.sql`, `docker compose up -d --build api` (server changed). The Android app needs a new build (`GOOGLE_SERVICES_JSON` secret set first if push is wanted) and reinstalling on any phone that already has the old one, same as every other `?v=` bump.

**Still open from the same review, not yet built:**
- No iOS project exists at all (no Capacitor iOS platform, no Xcode project, no Apple Developer account, no TestFlight) -- everything in `mobile/`/`docs/ANDROID_RELEASE.md` is Android-only.
- Apple's "wrapped website" rejection risk (Guideline 4.2) is uneven across the apps: POS and AUZsMob are genuinely offline-first, which is real cover; Payroll and Accounting are explicitly server-authoritative (see their own sections above), closer to "just a website in a frame" from a reviewer's point of view.
- The offline fallback page (`mobile/build.mjs`'s generated `www/index.html`) is a dead end ("you're offline, retry"), not a real degraded-but-working shell.
- No deep links -- password-reset/email-verification/WhatsApp-invoice links open in the phone's regular browser, never the installed app.
- Background sync is weaker inside a WebView than on an open browser tab, since the offline-first sync model assumes the page's own JS keeps running.
- Still never run on a real device or emulator -- no Android SDK exists in this sandbox; the cloud GitHub Action build (and now a real phone) are the only things that have ever actually compiled or installed it.

## Local-first hardening pass (see `LOCAL-FIRST-IMPLEMENTATION.md`)
Audit found AUZsPOS and AUZsMob already offline-first (IndexedDB + outbox + idempotent RPCs); Ledger and Pay stay server-authoritative on purpose. Fixed instead of rewritten:
- **AUZsMob outbox dropped queued sales on a server 5xx / unreachable server** (only `401` or `navigator.onLine === false` kept the queue). `mob/sync.js` now keeps the write on any *transient* failure (`isTransient`: no status, >= 500, 408, 429) with exponential back-off, and only rolls back a real 4xx refusal. The sync pill is tappable ("Not synced (n) - tap to retry").
- **`MBxxx` SQL error codes are HTTP 400** (`server/src/index.js`), so a business refusal is never mistaken for an outage. Any new module with its own error-code prefix must be added to that regex.
- POS: back-off after failed syncs (`retryAt`/`retryMs`), tappable status, and invoice/KOT numbering gives up after 2.5 s (`withTimeout`) so a dead server never stalls billing.
- AUZsMob registers `sw.js` itself (before, only the POS did, so it could not cold-start offline).
- Suite `node tests/run-local.mjs offline` (10 checks: 503, refused connection, lost reply, restart, refusal, retry pill; POS 503 / lost reply / restart / numbering). In Playwright tests the browser reaches `api.auzslab.in` through a host mapping, so a route handler cannot `route.fetch()` it: replay the request from the test runner with `rpc(stack, ...)` instead, and let the route handle only the real POST (`OPTIONS` preflight must `continue()`).
- Not done: offline Ledger/Pay drafts, direct ESC/POS printing, object storage, performance numbers, a single `POST /sync` endpoint (reasons in the doc).

## Public site must not describe how the backend works (owner instruction)
Marketing pages (`site/*.html`) never mention hosting model, infrastructure, tenants/"tenant structure", subdomain mechanics, databases, or which internal tools we run (Sentry, Resend, ...). Say "your own business address" / "private workspace" / "we look after it for you", never "self-hosted", "infrastructure we run", "tenant". The privacy policy may name data categories and say "an email provider" / "an error-monitoring provider". Do not put internal notes (`*.md`, TODO files) in `site/` or `app/public/`: both folders are served to the world (`site/REFERENCE_BUILD_TODO.md` was publicly downloadable until removed). Browser JS is downloadable by nature; protection is server-side authorization, not hiding code.

## Reading invoices and menus from PDF / photo (`ds/docread.js`, free, on the device)
- `ds/docread.js` (`auzDocRead.extract / parseInvoice / parseMenu`) reads a PDF with its own text through PDF.js (`vendor/pdfjs/`), and photos or scanned PDFs through Tesseract OCR (`vendor/ocr/`, English model, ~11 MB, loaded only when used; the OCR prepares the picture first: bigger, black and white, table grid lines removed). No server, no API key, no cost; nothing leaves the phone. The parsers are plain heuristics (GSTIN, "Invoice No", dates, a table row = description + qty + rate + amount, tax % from CGST/SGST/IGST rows; menu = capital/short lines are categories, a line ending in a price is an item, `100/150` = two sizes) and always return `notes` listing what looks doubtful.
- **It never saves by itself.** Accounting: Purchases -> "Scan invoice" and Expenses -> "Scan bill" (`scanInvoice()` in `accounts/p-docs.js`) open the normal bill/expense editor pre-filled (`applyScan` / `scanBanner` in `p-editor.js`: supplier matched by GSTIN or name, else an "Add this supplier" button; products matched by exact name; price-includes-tax detected) and the saved document gets the original file attached. Console: Menu -> Items -> "Read menu file" (`readMenuFile` / `menuDraft` in `console/p-menu.js`) shows an editable draft (category, name, price, type, tick to include); Create adds categories and items (an item that already exists in that category has its price updated). Recipes / raw materials are NOT read from a menu; they are added per item afterwards.
- Photo reading is weaker than PDF text (handwriting, angled or blurry shots will be wrong): the banner says so. If real files turn out too messy, an AI reader is the next step (needs an API key and a per-use cost: owner decision).
- Test: `node tests/run-local.mjs docread` (7 checks: parsers, a real Chromium-made PDF and PNG read in Accounting through to a saved bill with the file attached, a menu PDF to items in the console). Bump `?v=` of the files you change; `sw.js` precaches `ds/docread.js` (not the vendor files).
- Deploy: `git pull` only (static). Not yet built: POS/AUZsMob purchase-expense scanning, supplier-specific templates, item recipes from a recipe sheet.

## AUZsPay offline clock-in (db/120, `payroll/p-me.js`)
- The one offline exception in Payroll (everything else stays server-authoritative). With no connection, or a request that gets no answer at all (no HTTP status), the big clock button calls `keepOffline()`: it saves `{op, at, lat, lng, accuracy}` in `localStorage['pay.punchq']` with the phone's real time, shows a toast, and Today shows the last good `pay_me` (cached in `localStorage['pay.me']`, flagged `_offline`) with an "You are offline" banner. `flushPunchQueue()` sends the queue in order through `pay_me_punch_offline` on the `online` event, every 60 s and 3 s after load: success removes the item; a refusal (4xx) removes it and toasts the reason; no answer / 5xx / 401 / 408 / 429 stops and keeps the rest.
- **`pay_me_punch_offline(p jsonb)`** (db/120, registered in `server/src/index.js`): requires `op` (<= 64 chars), idempotent per business (`pay_punches.client_op`, unique where not null; a replay returns the state plus `duplicate:true`), records the punch at the phone's `at` (refused if more than 2 min in the future or older than 3 days), decides in/out on the server from the previous non-voided punch (after an `in` comes an `out`), same eligibility and geofence block/flag rules as `pay_me_punch`, source `mobile`, refreshes attendance for the day. The result is flat (`open`, `kind`, `recorded_at`, `duplicate`).
- Test: payroll suite ("Offline clock-in", 33 checks). The browser-side queue itself has not been driven in a real browser test, only the server function. Bump `?v=` of `p-me.js` (v2) and `V` in `sw.js` when changing it.
- Deploy: `psql ... -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 < db/120_pay_offline_punch.sql`, `docker compose up -d --build api`, `git pull`.

## SESSION LOG, latest: offline-first finish + invoice/menu reading (PR #222-227)
1. **Local-first hardening (PR #222-225):** AUZsMob outbox no longer drops queued sales on 5xx / no answer, `MB###` errors are HTTP 400, POS back-off and 2.5 s numbering timeout, AUZsMob registers `sw.js`, tappable sync status, offline test suite (10 checks). Wording that described the backend was removed from the public site and a publicly served internal notes file was deleted (see the "Public site must not describe how the backend works" rule). Full detail in `LOCAL-FIRST-IMPLEMENTATION.md`.
2. **AUZsPay offline clock-in (PR #226, db/120):** section above.
3. **Invoice / menu reading from PDF or photo (PR #227):** section "Reading invoices and menus from PDF / photo" above. Vendored `app/public/vendor/pdfjs/` and `vendor/ocr/` (about 13 MB, MIT/Apache licences included); only `ds/docread.js` is precached by `sw.js`.
4. **Server migrations on the VPS** were applied through db/119 (plus 112) and caddy restarted; **db/120 is the next one to run**. Order for a fresh deploy of everything after 116: 116, 117, 118, 119, 120.

### Tests (sandbox notes)
- Suites: marketing, responsive, journeys, salon, cafe, pos, apps, accounts, payroll, mobile, offline, docread, signup, design, security, load, loadmob. One at a time (shared test DB); `service postgresql start` first.
- In this sandbox the Sentry CDN is unreachable, so `accounts` shows 3 failures (two "request failed: browser.sentry-cdn.com" and a dark-mode contrast check) that are identical on an untouched `main`; they are the network, not the code. The docread suite filters Sentry errors out on purpose.

Never invent reviews.
