Not built for reading: POS / AUZsMob purchase and expense scanning, recipes from a recipe sheet, an AI reader for messy files.
- Sentry error review needs the owner's screenshots.

## Chapter One (cafe, Jodhpur, Instagram `chapterone_india`): a bespoke site, handover-ready, no tenant yet

A real prospective client, found the same way Showoff Salon was. The owner's plan: sell them **AUZsPOS as-is** (no customisation needed there) plus one genuinely custom piece -- **a fully bespoke website** for their QR table-ordering/booking, not a generic QR-menu page, built from design references the owner supplied (a product-hero layout, an item-detail-with-"you may also like" layout, a dark "About us" layout, and a postage-stamp invoice style) in the client's own olive-green/cream identity. **No real tenant has been provisioned** (no owner email yet) -- but the owner explicitly asked for a single real link that goes live with only a few changes, not a disposable demo, so this is built that way.

- **`app/public/chapterone/`** is a standalone, one-off bespoke build -- same precedent as `app/public/salon/` for Showoff Salon: this client's own look, not retrofitted into `site.html`/`ds/auz.css`, maintained on its own going forward. **It is a real multi-page site, not one long scroll**: `index.html` (hero, a 3-scene "Ground fresh / Poured slow / Served warm" sticky-stacking section, feature strip, the menu grid, founder/story, reservation form, takeaway cart) links every menu item to its own real sub-page, `item.html?id=<id>` -- a genuine bookmarkable/shareable URL per item showing its photo, description, a size/variant pill picker when the item has `sizes`, a quantity stepper, and up to 3 related items, matching the Cafetio item-detail reference the owner supplied. `shared.js` holds everything both pages need (menu loading, the cart, order/reservation submission, the UPI QR, sheet helpers) as plain globals loaded before each page's own script (`app.js` for `index.html`, `item.js` for `item.html`) -- same "no modules, no build step" convention the rest of this codebase uses for one app's files sharing state across its own pages. `style.css` stays one file (not split mobile/desktop -- a one-off brand site, not the shared design system). `invoice.html` (postage-stamp receipt), `manifest.json` + icons.
- **The real logo, used exactly as the owner supplied it, never redrawn.** `logo.jpg` is the owner's original file, untouched. `logo-full.png` (PIL-cropped to just the mark + wordmark) is what the header/footer/nav actually render (`filter:invert(1) brightness(1.6)` where it needs to sit light-on-dark). `icon-180.png`/`icon-512.png` are generated from the same source (the arch+sprig glyph alone, recoloured cream, composited onto an olive tile -- the same "ink tile + cream mark" convention every other AUZslab app icon uses) for the home-screen icon and manifest; `icon.svg` stays as the maskable fallback. An earlier hand-drawn SVG recreation of the logo, built before the owner supplied the real file, was deleted once the real one arrived -- never resurrect it.
- **The cart persists across pages**, not just within one: `shared.js` keeps it in `localStorage` (`chapterone_cart`), keyed `itemId|size` so the same item in two sizes is two separate lines (same convention `site.html`'s own QR ordering already uses for variants) -- navigating `index.html` -> `item.html` -> back keeps the cart intact.
- **`TENANT_SLUG` is already set to the real intended slug (`'chapterone'`), not blank.** `isUnprovisioned(error)` recognises the exact `'unknown tenant'` error `place_order`/`call_waiter` raise as their first check (db/018) and is the ONLY error treated as "not live yet, fall back to a local demo confirmation" -- any other server refusal is shown for real once the tenant exists. **This is exactly why a real order placed on this site today never shows up as a live order in any POS**: no tenant with slug `chapterone` has been provisioned yet, so every `place_order` call hits "unknown tenant" and falls back to the local demo confirmation by design, not a bug -- it goes properly live the instant `site/admin.html` provisions a tenant with this exact slug (niche `cafe`, AUZsPOS + AUZsPOS QR ticked), no code change, no redeploy.
- **The console's "Website & Booking" page (`mgmt/website` in `console/p-site.js`) now actually reaches this site.** `shared.js`'s `loadLiveMenu()` captures `public_menu`'s own `cfg` object and calls an `applyBranding(cfg)` hook (defined in `app.js`) that fills the hero kicker/title/subheading, the story section, and the footer about/hours/address/Instagram link from the owner's own `siteKicker`/`siteTag`/`siteSub`/`siteAbout`/`siteHours`/`addr`/`siteInsta` fields -- the exact fields that page already edits for every tenant. Its "Preview ↗" button now also points at `/chapterone/index.html` instead of the generic `/site.html` for this one tenant (`window.TENANT_SLUG=='chapterone'` check), so Preview actually shows the real bespoke site.
- **Preview opened a permanently blank white Safari tab for the owner, every time, even after closing and reopening the console -- a known iOS WebKit limitation, not a code bug in the page it opened.** The console's own manifest (`manifest-console.json`) is `display:"standalone"`, and iOS Safari has a long-standing bug where a `target="_blank"` link tapped from inside a standalone home-screen web app opens a new Safari tab that never actually loads anything -- the tab itself is stuck, independent of whatever the link points to (confirmed: `/chapterone/index.html` renders correctly on its own; the bug is in how iOS hands off the new-tab request from standalone mode, not in that page). Fixed by dropping `target="_blank"` from the Preview button so it navigates in the same window instead -- the one reliable workaround for this WebKit limitation. The console has several *other* `target="_blank"` links (WhatsApp, partner dashboard, uploaded-file links) that were left alone since nobody has reported this on them yet and `wa.me`/`tel:` links hand off to a different app regardless of the standalone-tab bug -- if a similar "tap it, get a stuck blank tab" report comes in for any of those, this is the same root cause and the same fix.
- **The logo and the hero/founder photos are editable from the console too, not just text.** Every `<img>` showing the logo on this site (header, nav overlay, footer, on both `index.html` and `item.html`) carries a shared `.co-logo` class; `shared.js`'s `applyLogo(cfg)` (called from `loadLiveMenu()` on every page, not just `app.js`'s page-specific hook) swaps all of them to the tenant's own `settings.logo` the moment the owner uploads one from Management -> Configuration -> Logo -- the same field every other tenant's own site already reads. `.co-logo-dark` marks the two usages drawn light-on-dark via `filter:invert(1) brightness(1.6)` (meant for the owner's dark line-art logo on a light background); that filter is dropped the instant a real uploaded logo is in play, since inverting an arbitrary photo/logo would wreck its real colours. `app.js`'s `applyBranding(cfg)` additionally swaps `#heroMark` to `siteHero` and `#founderAvatar` to `siteAboutImg` (Website & Booking's existing "Hero photo"/"About-section photo" uploaders) once either is set. Until the owner uploads anything, every image keeps rendering exactly as supplied (the owner's real `logo-full.png`, the drawn hero mark, the "C" monogram) -- nothing changes for a tenant who never touches these fields.
- **Verified with a real round-trip delay, not an instantly-resolving mock.** A first Playwright pass that resolved the `public_menu` RPC synchronously (zero latency) made it look like `applyBranding` never ran -- `app.js` is the *second* script tag, loaded after `shared.js` already called `loadLiveMenu()`, so a response arriving before the browser finishes fetching+executing `app.js` would find `applyBranding` still undefined and silently skip it (`typeof applyBranding === 'function'`). Re-run with even a 30ms delay (closer to any real network RPC) showed every field -- logo, hero photo, founder photo, headline, about text -- updating correctly, confirming this is the same pre-existing, network-latency-dependent pattern `siteKicker`/`siteTag` already relied on before this change, not a new bug. Worth knowing if `applyBranding` or `applyLogo` ever seem to silently not fire during a local/mocked test.
- **Linked both ways, per the brief ("link directly to their POS... accessible through website and booking option in their POS")**: the site's footer has a "Staff login" link to `/index.html` (the POS); the POS's own More page (`pos/staff.js`) gets an "Our website" business-tools row (gated on `window.TENANT_SLUG=='chapterone'`, same pattern as its Payroll/AUZsMob/Accounting rows) linking back to `/chapterone/index.html`. The POS's existing Reservations tab (`pos/reserve.js`, already gated on `isRestaurant()`) is the "booking option in their POS" asked for -- nothing new needed there, it already works once the tenant exists.
- **Table-QR dine-in (`?t=<id>` in the URL) is a separate, simpler path from takeaway**: a banner names the table and offers "Call waiter" (db/045/048); checkout goes straight to "Send to kitchen" with **no payment step** (already seated, bill settled with staff as normal). `guest_orders.tbl` is plain text, so whatever string a table's QR encodes is exactly what staff see, no table-list sync needed.
- **Takeaway requires payment before the order is sent** (owner's explicit ask): cart -> pickup-time picker -> a pay-to-confirm sheet with a UPI QR (same manual pattern `site/cart.html` uses while Razorpay is off) -> only on "I've paid" does `place_order` fire.
- **Verified in a real headless browser (Playwright, mocked `sb-client.js`/`config.js` so the site runs in demo mode with zero real network calls)**: the full multi-page flow end to end -- homepage menu grid -> a real navigation to `item.html?id=...` -> the size-pill picker changing the shown price -> related items linking to other real item pages -> add-to-cart -> the cart persisting back on `index.html` with the correct badge and drawer line -- plus the full takeaway-to-payment-to-confirmation flow and the table-QR banner/dine-in path. Zero JS errors (the only console noise was Google Fonts preconnect failing under this sandbox's restricted egress, same category as this file's other "the network is restricted here, not the app" notes).
- **Open, waiting on the owner:** the founder's real name/photo, real menu/pricing beyond the demo catalogue, and the owner's email for `provision_tenant` once ready to go live.
- **Real bug fixed: the homepage never redrew once a live menu arrived.** `shared.js`'s `loadLiveMenu()` calls an `onMenuLoaded()` hook if one exists after swapping `CATS`/`ITEMS` to the tenant's real catalogue -- `item.js` had one, `app.js` (the homepage) never did. The moment this tenant actually got provisioned with a real menu, the homepage silently kept showing the demo catalogue forever (nothing ever told it to redraw), so every menu card still linked to a demo item id (`item.html?id=cb`, etc.) -- `item.html` itself correctly switches to the live catalogue, looks that demo id up in it, finds nothing, and shows "not found." This was never a race condition, it was a guaranteed miss the instant a real menu existed. Fixed by adding `app.js`'s own `onMenuLoaded()` (resets `activeCat` to the first real category, redraws tabs and the grid). `item.js`'s not-found branch also now clears `#relatedGrid` (it previously left a stale related-items row from whichever catalogue rendered first, visible under the "not found" message in the owner's own screenshot).
- **The logo is a real transparent PNG now, not a cropped photo with a background still baked in.** The earlier `logo-full.png` was an opaque crop of the supplied photo (correct line art, but with the photo's own cream background as solid pixels) -- harmless on the site's own cream pages, but the `.co-logo-dark` CSS trick (`filter:invert(1)` to show the mark light-on-dark in the nav overlay and footer) inverts a *solid* light background into a solid near-black rectangle, which is exactly the dark box the owner's screenshot caught around "chapter1" in the footer. Rebuilt from the same source photo with a real alpha channel (the background colour sampled and knocked out to transparency, ink colour held constant so anti-aliased edges don't halo) -- the same CSS invert trick now only inverts the visible ink pixels, leaving true transparency everywhere else, so there is no box on any background. `icon-180.png`/`icon-512.png` were regenerated the same way (the arch+sprig glyph cropped from the new alpha source, recoloured cream, composited onto the olive tile) instead of the earlier hand-tweaked pixel recolour.
- **Palette changed twice -- it is now the main AUZslab marketing site's own tokens (`site/theme.css`), not a cafe-specific swatch.** First pass used the owner's reference swatch (white/peach/soft coral/cream/olive). Owner then asked for the marketing site's own white/black/wine identity instead: off-white `#faf9f7` background, white `#ffffff` cards, near-black ink `#2e2c2a` (`site/theme.css`'s `--ink`) for text/accents/buttons, wine `#800020` (`site/theme.css`'s `--accent`) for prices and CTAs. CSS variable *names* in `style.css` stayed `--cream`/`--olive`/`--rust` (least code churn) even though the colours they hold are now white/ink/wine, not cream/olive/rust -- say so in a comment if you touch this again, so the names don't mislead the next session. Every hardcoded hex that repeated the old values directly (not just the CSS variables) was updated together across `style.css`, `index.html`, `item.html`, `invoice.html` and the bespoke branch of the shared `i.html` invoice (`db/124`, the one rendering this tenant's real POS bills). The owner's own logo artwork keeps its own baked-in ink colour throughout (per "use the exact logo, do not change it") -- only the site chrome around it (buttons, headings, backgrounds) follows the palette.
- **A palette or CSS content edit that doesn't bump the `?v=` on the stylesheet link is invisible to a returning browser.** The first palette pass shipped and deployed correctly, but `index.html`/`item.html` kept linking `style.css?v=2` -- a browser that had ever loaded that exact URL before kept serving its cached copy, surviving a server-side deploy and even a hard refresh, since the URL itself never changed. Fixed by bumping to `?v=3` (then `?v=4` for this entry's own changes). **Any edit to `chapterone/style.css` or `chapterone/app.js` must bump its `?v=` query string on every page that links it, not just update the file** -- there is no service worker or build step here to do this automatically, unlike the POS/Payroll/Accounting apps' `sw.js` precache + `?v=` convention.
- **Homepage redesign (owner screenshot + written brief): a big hero photo panel, the three "Ground / Poured / Served" scroll scenes paired with photos in a zigzag (photo-left/text-right, then text-left/photo-right, then photo-left/text-right), and a rounded (not flush-rectangle) footer.** `#heroArt` (around `#heroMark`) is now a large rounded panel (`min-height:460px`, `border-radius:28px`); once the owner uploads a Hero photo (Website & Booking -> Hero photo, same `siteHero` field as before), `applyBranding()` in `app.js` adds `.has-photo` so the image fills the panel edge-to-edge instead of floating small and centred -- until then it keeps showing the logo mark in a bigger frame, not a blank photo placeholder. The three scenes' markup was restructured into a `.bphoto`/`.btext` pair per scene (scene 1 and 3: photo div first in the DOM, so it reads on the left on desktop; scene 2: text div first, photo on the right) with a `max-width:899px` media query collapsing to one column and always showing the photo first on phones regardless of desktop side. The photos themselves reuse the **existing** Gallery photos field (Website & Booking -> Gallery photos, `siteGallery`, comma-joined URLs) in upload order for steps 1/2/3 -- no new upload field was invented; each `.bphoto` keeps its original decorative line-art SVG icon as the fallback in any slot that has no gallery photo yet. **Uploaded images are already auto-compressed**: every Website & Booking upload (hero, about, gallery, logo) already goes through `console/core.js`'s `compressImage(file,1400,.8)` before it ever reaches storage (resizes to a 1400px-wide JPEG at 80% quality) -- this was true before this change and needed no new code for the "auto-compress on upload" ask. Footer: `margin:0 16px 16px;border-radius:32px` (`22px`/`8px` margin on phones) so it floats as a rounded card above the page edge instead of a flush rectangle.
- **Verified in a real headless browser** at 1440px and 390px: zigzag order correct in both directions (step 1/3 photo-left, step 2 photo-right, desktop), photo-first stacking on phone, rounded hero panel and footer card rendering, zero JS errors (only the usual Google Fonts network-restriction noise in this sandbox).

## A paused task stays paused until the owner un-pauses it, even across stop-hook nagging

`stop-hook-git-check.sh` fires on *any* uncommitted change, including work the owner explicitly told a session to stop and leave alone mid-build. That hook firing is not the owner speaking -- never treat it as permission to commit, discard, or otherwise touch paused work. If an unrelated task needs committing while a paused task's files sit uncommitted in the same tree, `git stash push -u` the paused files, do the unrelated commit, then `git stash pop` to restore them exactly as left.

## Two real traps hit while shipping the Chapter One commit above -- re-check both before every commit

1. **The working tree can be checked out to a stale branch inherited from earlier, unrelated work**, not the branch this session was actually told to develop on. `git branch --show-current` before every commit, not just at session start. If a commit lands on the wrong branch: `git checkout <correct-branch>`, `git cherry-pick <the-commit>`, push from there, then `git reset --hard origin/<wrong-branch>` to remove the stray commit (safe precisely because it was never pushed from there).
2. **`git push -u origin <intended-branch>` does not fail loudly if a same-named local branch already exists but is on a different commit** -- it pushes *that* branch's existing (unrelated) state instead of the branch actually checked out, prints "Everything up-to-date," and gives no sign anything is wrong. Always confirm with `git log -1 --oneline` against both the local and `origin/<branch>` ref after any push whose correctness matters.
3. **A designated branch can have its own earlier PR already squash-merged into `main` without this session knowing** -- `git merge-base --is-ancestor <branch>~N origin/main` catches it. When it has, restart the branch from `origin/main` (`git checkout -B <branch> origin/main`) and cherry-pick only the commits still unmerged, rather than fighting a merge conflict against the branch's own stale history (a stale `CLAUDE.md` on one side and the current ~1400-line one on the other produces a conflict spanning hundreds of lines that is not meaningfully resolvable by a text merge -- redo the edit by hand against the current file instead).

Accounting needs a connection to save and post.'" -- citing gapless document numbers, period locks and double-entry balancing as the reason. ## Login-email change + recovery email, self-service and admin override (db/121)

Owner request: let a client change their own login email later (useful right after onboarding someone with a placeholder address, e.g. the owner's own email used to stand in for a prospective client's before their real one is known -- see the Chapter One section above), add a verified recovery/backup email, and give the platform admin a direct override for when a client's login email is simply wrong.

- **Reuses `email_verifications` (db/092) rather than a parallel table.** A new `purpose` column (`'verify'|'change'|'recovery'`, default `'verify'` so every existing row/behaviour is unaffected) means a link minted for one flow can never be replayed to complete a different one -- `confirmEmailVerification` was tightened to filter `purpose='verify'` explicitly, matching the discipline the two new confirm functions use from the start.
- **A login email is never actually changed until the NEW address proves it can receive mail** -- the confirmation link is mailed only to the new address, never the old one, same "don't trust an unverified email" rule as db/069's Google/Apple pre-hijack guard. `server/src/auth.js`: `verifyPassword`, `emailInUse`, `createEmailChange`/`confirmEmailChange`, `createRecoveryEmailVerification`/`confirmRecoveryEmail`/`removeRecoveryEmail`. New routes in `server/src/index.js`: `/auth/change-email/request` (needs the current password, same bar `deleteOwnAccount` uses), `/auth/change-email/confirm`, `/auth/recovery-email/request|confirm|remove`. `server/src/mail.js` gained `sendEmailChangeVerification`/`sendRecoveryEmailVerification`, same dormant-until-`RESEND_API_KEY`-is-set discipline as every other mail sender here -- when mail isn't configured, the link comes back directly in the response (`invite_staff`'s existing fallback pattern) so the page can show it to copy/paste.
- **A recovery email is a verified backup contact only -- it can never sign in and never substitutes for the real forgot-password flow.** Confirmed by test: logging in with the recovery email as the username returns 401.
- **`my_dashboard()` (db/013) gained `my_recovery_email`/`my_recovery_email_verified`** -- `site/account.html`'s existing "Your login" card grew a "Change email" toggle-open form, and a new "Recovery email" card handles add/remove.
- **Platform admin override**: `admin_set_user_email(p_user_id, p_new_email)` (security definer, `admin_log`'d like every other admin action in db/087) sets a client's email immediately, no verification link needed -- same trust level as `admin_set_user_disabled`/`admin_delete_user`. `site/admin.html`'s user detail modal gained a "Change login email" button (hidden for platform-admin accounts, same exclusion `userDeleteBtn` already uses), `prompt()`-based like the existing delete-permanently flow.
- **Registered in `server/src/index.js`'s `RPC` allow-list** (`admin_set_user_email`) -- this file's own repeated lesson about a function existing in Postgres but 404ing over HTTP until it's added there.
- **Verified against a real running server, not just the SQL**: wrong password refused (401), malformed email refused (400), an email already taken by another account refused (409) both for the self-service request and for the admin override, the OLD email still signs in right up until the link is opened, the link changes it and the OLD email then gets 401 while the NEW one works, a used confirm token cannot be replayed, `my_dashboard` reflects the change, a recovery email is not set until its own link is confirmed and cannot sign in once it is, removing it clears both columns, and a non-admin cannot call the admin override. Also driven through the real browser UI (Playwright against the real local test server, not mocked): the account.html change-email/recovery-email forms and the admin.html "Change login email" button and modal refresh, end to end.
- Deploy: `git pull`, `psql ... -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 < db/121_email_change_and_recovery.sql`, `docker compose up -d --build api` (server changed).

## DPDP Act 2023 support: consent record, my data, rights requests (db/122, `docs/DPDP.md`)
- **Sign-up consent:** `site/signup.html` (sign-up mode only) shows a required, unticked "I agree to the Privacy policy and Terms" box plus an optional "product updates" box; Google (and Apple) are blocked until the first is ticked (`consentGate()`). The choice rides the redirect (`signin.html?...&consent=1&upd=0|1`, kept in the OAuth `state` as `c`) and `site/signin.html` records it against the new account with `record_my_consent` before handing the session back. Log in does not show the box.
- **Tables** (db/122, RLS on, no policies, only SECURITY DEFINER functions): `user_consents` (notice version + purposes `{service,updates}` + source), `data_requests` (access / correction / erasure / withdraw / nominee / grievance / other; open -> in_progress -> done|rejected; `acknowledged_at`, `due_at` = 30 days). Functions (all registered in `server/src/index.js`): `record_my_consent`, `my_consents`, `withdraw_my_consent`, `submit_data_request` (max 5 open), `my_data_requests`, `my_personal_data` (never hashes/tokens), `privacy_current_version`, admin: `admin_list_data_requests`, `admin_update_data_request` (reply required to close; audited via `admin_log`), `admin_open_data_request_count`.
- **Re-consent when the policy changes:** `platform_flags.privacy_version` (now `2026-10`, matching "Last updated" in `site/privacy.html`). Bump both together; every signed-in person then gets a banner (`checkConsent()` in `site/account.html`) until they agree.
- **UI:** `site/account.html` "Privacy & my data" tab (`#privacy`, shown to customers with no business too): consent, optional-updates switch, download my data, send a request, list of requests with our replies. `site/admin.html` "Privacy requests" section (`privacyReq` in `SECTION_LOADERS`, badge from `admin_open_data_request_count`). Nobody is emailed automatically: the admin must check the queue.
- **A real bug fixed on the way:** `site/site.js` (all three copies: `site/`, `site/kept/`, `site/tool/`) turned every clicked `<a href>` into a page transition, including programmatic `blob:` download links, so account.html's "Backup" and the help screenshot opened the file as a page instead of downloading. Anchors with `download` or a `blob:`/`data:` href are now left alone (all pages' `site.js?v=` bumped).
- Test: `node tests/run-local.mjs dpdp` (11 checks: consent per version, requests, limits, my-data content, admin queue + audit, cascade on deletion, sign-up gate, account tab, download, admin screen). Deploy: `psql ... -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 < db/122_dpdp.sql`, `docker compose up -d --build api` (new RPCs), `git pull`.

Details in `LOCAL-FIRST-IMPLEMENTATION.md` items 9-13. `POST /sync` (AUZsMob uses it), `ds/escpos.js` direct receipt printing (Chrome/Edge/Android Chrome only, untested on real printers), AUZsLedger offline DRAFTS (`accounts/offline.js`, `acc_save_draft_offline`, `acc_documents.client_op`; never posts offline; add new Accounting files to `sw.js` too, the offline suite checks the list), optional S3 for private files (`server/src/s3.js`, dormant without `S3_*`). ## Two kinds of client at onboarding: Standard and Custom-built (db/125)
Admin -> Onboard new (`site/admin.html`) first asks **Standard** or **Custom-built**. Standard = pick a niche plus the standard products (ticking none keeps the niche defaults; ticked products are applied with `admin_set_tenant_features`, same `SELLABLE` list as Clients -> Edit). Custom-built = a specially designed system made to the client's demand: `provision_custom_tenant(name, slug, brief)` creates a tenant with `tenants.build_type='custom'`, niche `custom` (preset with NO features, `bizType:'custom'`), the brief saved in the client's notes, plus the usual own address and owner login. The Clients list shows "Custom-built" and the Edit sheet hides the standard-products checklist for them. A custom client's bare address opens `/custom.html` (a holding page, via `LANDING` in `land.html`); the real custom app is then built for them like Showoff Salon / Chapter One (`app/public/<name>/`, wire it into `LANDING` and Caddy as needed). `provision_tenant` sets `build_type` too (retrying an unfinished onboarding with the other type switches it). `app/public/admin/onboard.html` (older form) still only does standard. Deploy: `psql ... -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 < db/125_custom_build_clients.sql`, `docker compose up -d --build api` (new RPC registered), `git pull`. Test: journeys suite ("Onboarding has two kinds").

## Agents Office (owner's AI team) — separate app, opened from the admin panel
A separate app that lives in `agents-office/` of this repo (own Dockerfile, login, key and notes; it shares no code, database or API with AUZslab; an own GitHub repo was not possible from the build sandbox): a dashboard where the owner types a task, the right AI agent (5 pods of 5 = 25 agents) drafts it from a folder of Markdown notes, and the result is saved back as a note. Drafts only: agents have no tools and cannot send, post, pay or delete; anything acting outside the app waits for the owner's tap. It has its own password, its own Anthropic key and its own notes folder, and no access to this database or any customer data (that is why it was kept out of the main API).
- **Wiring here:** admin panel sidebar + More list have an "Agents Office" link (`#agentsLinkSide` / `#agentsLinkMore`) opening `https://agents.auzslab.in` in the same window (no `target="_blank"`: an iOS home-screen app opens those as a permanently blank tab). `Caddyfile` has an `agents.{$DOMAIN}` block (`reverse_proxy agents-office:3100`, same pattern as `status.`), `docker-compose.yml` has the `agents-office` service behind the **`agents` profile** (build context `./agents-office`, env from `agents-office/.env`, brain bind-mounted), so a normal `docker compose up -d` never touches it and never fails if that folder is missing. `db/126_reserve_agents_slug.sql` reserves the `agents` business address.
- **Start it:** `docker compose --profile agents up -d --build agents-office`, then `docker compose restart caddy` (steps in `agents-office/README.md`). Until started, `agents.auzslab.in` answers 502.
- Not verified here: the Docker build/run and the Caddy block (no Docker daemon in the sandbox); the app itself has 20 tests and a real-browser UI check (phone and desktop).
- Design note: its "floor" view (drawn top-down office, department stats, live task panel) was inspired by a third-party product (Sahni.ai Agents Office, PolyForm Noncommercial, not for resale). Our version is our own drawing and code; do not copy that product's code or assets.
- Deploy: `git pull`, `psql ... < db/126_reserve_agents_slug.sql`, then the two commands above.

### Agents Office: what is in it, and how its first deploy went wrong (read before touching `.env`)
- **Roster:** `agents-office/agents.json` = 5 pods x 5 agents (25): Engineering (UI/UX, Coding, Code Review, Bug Detection, Performance), Operations & Security (DevOps, Server Monitoring, Cybersecurity, Database Monitoring, Backup & Disaster Recovery), Product & Delivery (Product Manager, QA/Testing, Release/Deployment, Product Research, Documentation), Customers & Growth (Customer Support, Customer Onboarding, Sales/Lead Generation, Marketing, SEO), Business & Insight (Business Analytics, Data Analysis, Financial/SaaS Metrics, Internal Knowledge, Operations/Workflow). Engineering and operations agents are draft-only: nothing runs on any server. Agents are idle until a task is added; a green dot / lit desk only while a task is being drafted. Needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in `agents-office/.env` (without it tasks fail; the login and floor still work). Cost estimate: roughly Rs 3-6 per task (the owner should set a monthly spending limit in the Anthropic console).
- **Its own `.env` is `agents-office/.env`** (never the repo-root `.env`): `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OFFICE_PASSWORD_HASH` (scrypt hash, **wrapped in single quotes** because it contains `$`; compose would otherwise interpolate it), `OFFICE_SESSION_SECRET`, `OFFICE_TRUST_PROXY=1`. Make the hash with `node -e "import('./lib/auth.js').then(m=>console.log(m.hashPassword(process.env.PW)))"` with `PW` set. A silent `read -s` prompt is unusable over Termius on a phone (the owner typed nothing and an empty password got hashed): use a visible `read -r -p`, check `${#PW}` is 12+, and `clear` the screen afterwards.
- **INCIDENT (2026-10-07), do not repeat: the root `.env` was overwritten with the blank template.** The README told the owner to `cd <folder> && cp .env.example .env`; the `cd` target did not exist yet, the `cp` ran in `/root/auzslabs-infra` and replaced the real `.env` (database passwords, `JWT_SECRET`, DNS token, ...) with placeholders. Nothing broke until `docker compose up -d --build api` recreated the API: it then failed with `password authentication failed for user "app"` (`POSTGRES_APP_PASSWORD` no longer matched the role created at first init) and the whole API crash-looped. **Recovery:** containers keep the env they were created with, and `docker compose restart` does not re-read `.env`, so the long-running `caddy` container still held the old values: `docker inspect auzslabs-infra-caddy-1 --format '{{json .Config.Env}}'` -> rebuild `.env` (script in the chat history: write `KEY='value'` per variable, skip PATH/XDG_*/HOSTNAME/HOME/TERM/CADDY_VERSION) -> `docker compose up -d --force-recreate api`. That only recovers what existed when caddy was created (about 7 days earlier), so settings added after that were lost and had to be restored by hand: `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` and `SENTRY_DSN` come back from `app/public/config.js` (public values), `MAIL_FROM` from `.env.example`; **`RESEND_API_KEY`, Razorpay, Cashfree, Turnstile, FCM and S3 values (if any were set) cannot be recovered and must be re-entered by the owner.** Rules from this: (1) never write `cp .env.example .env` in a deploy instruction without an absolute path and a `[ -f .env ] &&` guard; (2) the owner should keep a copy (`cp .env /root/env.backup-$(date +%F); chmod 600 /root/env.backup-*`) and a copy of every secret in a password manager; (3) after any change to `.env`, `docker compose config -q && echo CONFIG_OK` before recreating anything; (4) a half-fixed `.env` blocks every `docker compose` command, so fix by replacing the whole line (`sed -i "77s|.*|...|"`), never by re-running a wrapping `sed` (the same line was quoted three times when a block was pasted three times).
- **`source .env` breaks on an unquoted value with spaces or `<>`** (`MAIL_FROM=AUZslab <onboarding@resend.dev>`): bash stops sourcing at that line, so the variables after it are unset in the shell. Quote such values in `.env` (compose strips the quotes). Docker services read `.env` through `env_file`, not the shell, so this only affects the migration commands that use `$POSTGRES_USER` / `$POSTGRES_DB`.
- **Production had migrations that were never applied** (found only because the admin "Privacy requests" page showed a generic error and the API log said `relation "user_profiles" does not exist`): `db/103` was missing although 122 (which joins it) was applied, and `db/123` was also missing. Because the API now hides unexpected 500 details from the browser ("Something went wrong on our side"), **the real error is only in `docker compose logs api`**: always look there first. Check list (run on the server, prints ok/MISSING per marker): `to_regclass('user_profiles')` (103), `mob_data_epoch` function (110), `bundles.yearly_price` (113), `bundles.auto` (114), `subscriptions` (116), `auth_totp` (117), `fcm_tokens` (119), `pay_me_punch_offline` (120), `acc_save_draft_offline` (123), `provision_custom_tenant` (125). All of db/103-126 are idempotent (`if not exists` / `create or replace`) so a missing one can simply be run.
- **Caddy must be restarted after the Caddyfile changes** (`docker compose restart caddy`): until then `agents.auzslab.in` is caught by the wildcard business block and lands on the POS login. A blank white page with no text from a Caddy-fronted address usually means an upstream 502 (Caddy sends an empty body), i.e. the service behind it is not running: check `docker compose --profile agents ps` and `logs`.
- **Docker/Caddy path for this app is verified in production** (container started, `listening on 0.0.0.0:3100`, caddy routes the host), but the full sign-in -> task -> note flow has not yet been confirmed with a real Anthropic key.

## Security review pass (2026-10-07): what was found and changed
- **Rate limits added** (`server/src/index.js`): per-account login limit (30 / 15 min) on top of the per-IP one; password-reset mail at most 3 per address per hour (same reply either way); 2FA code guesses limited per sign-in challenge (8 / 10 min); password-checking RPCs (`change_my_password`, `reset_staff_password`, `staff_reset_pin`, `admin_reset_client_password`) limited per person (10 / 15 min), because a stolen session token could otherwise guess the current password through the RPC with no limit.
- **Sessions die when a password changes:** `resetPassword()` revokes every session of that user; `change_my_password` revokes all other sessions (keeps the current one); `reset_staff_password`, `staff_reset_pin` and `staff_set_active(false)` revoke the staff member's sessions (hooks after `callRpc` in the `/rpc` handler, because SQL cannot touch the in-memory revoked-jti set). `revokeAllSessionsForUser(userId, exceptJti)`.
- **`clientIp()` uses the LAST `X-Forwarded-For` entry** (the one Caddy wrote), never the first, which a caller could set to anything to dodge every rate limit.
- **`/staff/send-invite-email` was an open mail relay** (any owner could mail any address with any link): the link must now be on our own domain (`safeResetBase`) and an owner can send 30 a day.
- **Docker:** Caddy (internet-facing) gets only `DOMAIN`, `ACME_EMAIL`, `GODADDY_API_TOKEN` via `environment:`, no longer the whole `.env` (database passwords, `JWT_SECRET`, every API key). The off-site backup container gets only `B2_BUCKET` plus the two B2 values. The API has `no-new-privileges`. **After pulling this, recreate caddy and offsite-backup once** (`docker compose up -d --force-recreate caddy offsite-backup`); a plain restart keeps the old environment.
- **Caddyfile:** CSP now also sets `object-src 'none'; base-uri 'self'` (no `script-src`: the tool pages still use inline scripts, so a script policy would break them). New branded `site/404.html` via `handle_errors` on the root domain (still answers 404). Validate the Caddyfile locally with a stock `caddy` binary after removing the `dns godaddy` line (that plugin is only in the real image).
- **Checked and fine:** every public-schema table the generic `/db/:table` API can reach has RLS (the tables without it are server-only: auth_*, payments, subscriptions, phone_otps, platform_flags, product_prices, salon_store, ...); every `SECURITY DEFINER` function sets `search_path`; uploads accept only JPEG/PNG/WebP/PDF by magic bytes (no SVG), 5xx errors never leak internals, CORS reflects only our own domain, all internal links in `site/` resolve, `sitemap.xml` lists only real pages.
- **Known gaps at the time of this pass, since closed in the "Security cleanup batch" section below**: `password_resets.token` plaintext, `auth_totp.secret` plaintext, the API container running as root. **Still open:** owner/manager sessions are 7-day bearer tokens kept in `localStorage` (the XSS defence is `h()` never using `innerHTML`; a real `script-src` CSP needs the inline scripts moved out first); platform-admin accounts deliberately have no 2FA (see the 2FA section) so their password must be long and unique; Google/Apple sign-in does not go through the 2FA prompt.
- Tests added to the security suite: password change and password reset sign out other devices; the invite mail refuses a foreign link.

## Homepage sheet stack: adding a sheet needs a pin rule (fix after the Intelligence sheet)
The homepage's first `.stack` is sticky sheets styled by `.stack-panel:nth-of-type(N)` in `site/theme.css` (phone block near line 500, desktop block near line 400). **Only sheets 1 to 5 have a rule.** Adding a sixth sheet means adding `nth-of-type(6)` (with its `--tab-step` multiple and next z-index) in BOTH blocks, or it never stops and stack: it just scrolls under the others. Also keep each new sheet at or under one screen tall on a phone (about 844px); a taller sheet pins only once its bottom shows, so it scrolls up to the top instead of pausing partway (owner noticed). Measure with a headless script: `[...document.querySelectorAll('.stack-panel')].map(p=>p.offsetHeight)` at 390x844 and 1440x900. Current homepage sheets: hero, WORK, BUILD, KNOW (Intelligence), VIEW, then the CONNECT footer sheet in its own `.stack` (z-index 30, not sticky). ## Security cleanup batch: hashed reset tokens, encrypted TOTP secrets, non-root API container (db/134) -- plus a real 2FA bug found while fixing it

Closing four items this file had been carrying as "known gaps, need a decision or a migration" since the earlier security review pass.

- **`password_resets.token` is no longer stored in plaintext.** Mirrors `email_verifications` (db/092) exactly: `createPasswordReset()` now generates the raw token in Node (`randomBytes(32).toString('base64url')`), mails it, and stores only `sha256(token)` in a new `token_hash` column; `resetPassword()` hashes the incoming token the same way to match. `db/134_hash_password_reset_tokens.sql` drops the old plaintext `token` primary key and promotes `token_hash` to it -- since these rows expire in 1 hour, there was nothing worth backfilling, so the migration just expires every currently-outstanding link outright (`update ... set expires_at = now() where used_at is null`) rather than attempt to hash something already in flight.
- **`auth_totp.secret` is no longer stored in plaintext**, but with **no migration needed at all** -- the fix is entirely in `server/src/auth.js`. `encryptTotpSecret()`/`decryptTotpSecret()` use AES-256-GCM with a key derived from `JWT_SECRET` via HKDF into its own distinct subkey (`hkdfSync('sha256', JWT_SECRET, '', 'auzslab-auth-totp-secret-v1', 32)` -- never reusing `JWT_SECRET` directly, which stays scoped to signing). The stored format is `enc:<iv>:<tag>:<ciphertext>` (base64 each); `decryptTotpSecret()` checks for the `enc:` prefix first and returns the value as-is if absent -- a base32 TOTP secret can never start with `enc:` (lowercase + colon aren't in its alphabet), so this is an unambiguous, zero-downtime, backward-compatible switch: every new `generate2faSecret()` call encrypts, every existing plaintext row (if any real account had 2FA on before this) keeps working untouched and only gets upgraded to encrypted the next time that account's 2FA is set up again (disable + re-enable to upgrade sooner, same as the file's past precedent for similar low-stakes gaps).
- **The API container (`server/Dockerfile`) no longer runs as root.** `node:22-alpine` ships a built-in non-root `node` user (uid/gid 1000); the two upload volumes (`uploads_data`, `private_uploads_data`) are plain named Docker volumes Docker creates owned by root on first use, and an already-deployed instance's volumes are root-owned from running as root before this. `server/docker-entrypoint.sh` is the standard fix for exactly this (the same pattern official postgres/redis alpine images use): the container still starts as root for one moment, `chown -R node:node` on those two mount points, then `exec su-exec node "$@"` drops to the non-root user to actually run the server -- root is never the process serving a request. `apk add su-exec` added alongside the existing `fontconfig ttf-dejavu`. **Verified as far as this sandbox's network allows**: `apk add` itself couldn't be exercised (the sandbox's egress allowlist blocks `dl-cdn.alpinelinux.org`, same category as every other "network restricted here" note in this file), but the actual privilege-drop mechanism was verified directly against the real `node:22-alpine` base image pulled fresh: a root-owned file in a volume-mount-equivalent directory, chowned, then written to successfully as the `node` user via `su` (confirmed `id` shows uid 1000 and a write to `/root` is correctly refused) -- proving the ownership-fix-then-drop sequence actually works, only the one new `apk` package's installability in this exact sandbox is unconfirmed.
- **A real, previously undetected bug found and fixed while verifying the TOTP encryption end to end: `/auth/2fa/setup` gave every real owner/manager a 403, always.** Its own role check (`select role from profiles where id = $1`) used a bare `pool.query`, never wrapped in `withAuth()` -- `profiles` has owner-read-only RLS (db/002's `p_read` policy, `using (id = app_uid() or ...)`), and `app_uid()` is null without `app.uid` set, so the policy hid the row 100% of the time under the API's own `app` role. This is the exact same bug shape this file now documents a fourth time (`createOrder()`'s RLS read, `storage.js`'s `saveSiteUpload`, the `subscriptions` table) -- caught only by actually driving the real HTTP endpoint with a real owner login rather than trusting the 2FA section's own earlier "verified against a real running server" claim, which it turns out never exercised this specific call path end to end. Fixed with the same `withAuth(user.id, (client) => client.query(...))` wrap already used at the other `profiles` read sites in `index.js`. A committed regression test now drives the full real setup -> confirm -> login-challenge -> disable flow (independent RFC 6238 code computation, not reusing `totp.js`) in `tests/suites/local/security.mjs`.
- Deploy: `git pull`, `psql ... -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 < db/134_hash_password_reset_tokens.sql`, `docker compose up -d --build api` (Dockerfile and server/src both changed).
